## 🥇️ Why repo is a game changer

### 📈️ Requirements + Docs + Stats + Semantics

#### 🚀️ Agents love TDD

[Test-Driven-Development (TDD)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test-driven_development) is a game changer for agents 🚀️

##### ↔ Multi-lanugage development

Ever had the problem that you domain-expert devs write Python/C++/C/Javascript/Ruby/Lua/… but not Typescript/Go/Rust/C#/Julia/… or vice versa?

No problem, let them write what they know, until the tests are extended and let agents reimplement it natively until the tests succeeds ✅️

## Developer Operations

Start `repo dashboard` at the monorepo root. Choose **New Task** (`Ctrl+B n`),
search by action and owner, complete the command form and choose **Start**. The
[dashboard guide](🔨️modules/🎛️dashboard/README.md#-daily-work) gives concrete
setup, testing, debugging, source navigation and repository workflows. Every
command uses the workspace daemon, so its status, output and cancellation remain
available after closing a view or reconnecting the dashboard.

Open **Workspace Files** (`Ctrl+B F`) to browse and edit source in native document tabs.
Save with `Ctrl+B S`; inspect retained predecessors and bounded local history from
the same view. Executable commands are maintained in owner dashboard metadata or
project targets and selected in the dashboard.

## Configuration

Repo-wide settings live in [`.🧬semio/🦑️repo/config.toml`](../.🧬semio/🦑️repo/config.toml) at the monorepo root.

| Key                  | Default    | Description                                                                 |
| -------------------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `logging.session`    | `false`    | Write per-session `session.json` under `.🧬semio/🦑️repo/⚡️/🤖️/…` on agent hooks       |
| `logging.operations` | `true`     | Append derived `agent.<operation>.<phase>` events (requires `session = true`)      |
| `logging.plan`       | `true`     | Track agent plan steps in `session.json` (requires `session = true`)        |
| `logging.detail`     | `standard` | `minimal` (event only), `standard` (+ response), or `full` (+ native stdin) |

Set `logging.session = true` to enable session-file logging for debugging or coordinator ingestion.

# 🏗️ Build and run

`repo` is the only build entry. It is tool-neutral: every project target is declared once in a `📋️project.json`, a neutral graph describes all projects and targets, and two interchangeable implementations execute them: **Nx with Bun** (default) and **Bazel** (`--tool=bazel`). Never call `nx`, `bunx nx`, `npx nx`, `bazel`, raw `cargo` or `rustc` yourself. This is the rule of `AGENTS.md` that external tools sit behind a tool-independent interface and that the `repo` entry runs every command: `repo` is that interface for Nx, Bazel and Cargo. Paths that start with `📚️library/` are below `🔨️modules/` of this product. Start with the Build tools guide; the sections after it are the reference. A German version of this chapter follows its last section.

```bash
repo run <project>:<target>[:<configuration>]       # one task; add --tool=bazel for the Bazel implementation
repo run-many --targets=<targets> [--projects=<projects>]
repo affected --targets=<targets>                    # projects that changed against HEAD, plus their dependents
repo exec --projects=<projects> -- <command>         # a command once per project, project root as working directory
repo run <project>:<target> --dry-run                # print the task graph, run nothing
repo show projects|project <name>|targets            # the neutral graph (also: repo graph)
repo cache status [--tasks]|gc [--dry-run]|clear     # the cache ledger (--tasks: the task cache of the selected tool), its collection, and clearing the task cache
repo cache remote status                             # the remote cache contract, its rendering for the tool, whether the endpoint answers
repo cache serve [--port <n>]                        # serve a bounded remote cache for Bazel and Nx until stopped
repo tools setup [nx|bazel|all]                      # provision the pinned build tools for this host
repo dependencies sync --all                         # install dependencies (--scope <owner> for one owner)
repo dependencies lock --all                         # regenerate bun.lock after a manifest edit
repo dependencies ensure                             # install only when missing or changed (commands that need dependencies run this first)
repo capacity status                                 # free memory above the floor, running and waiting grants of the host
repo capacity run [--memory=<MB>] -- <command>       # run a heavy command under a machine-wide capacity grant
repo incremental list|plan|prepare|run|discard       # prove in a disposable clone that Nx and Bazel rebuild exactly what an edit affects
repo verify build-parity [--only=<ids|area:x>]       # prove that Nx and Bazel produce the same outputs
repo leases status [--json]                          # holders and waiters of leases, lock-order violations, deadlock victims
repo concurrency list|plan|run                       # prove that simulated agents never wait for unrelated projects
repo cargo --store=shared --configuration=prototype -- check -p <crate> --lib
```

- **Help.** `repo --help` prints every command and every flag; `repo <command> --help` (also with a subcommand, for example `cache clear --help`) prints that command's own usage, options and examples. The text is rendered from the operation catalogue in English or German: `--lang=en|de`, then `SEMIO_LANGUAGE`, then the locale (`LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES`, `LANG`), then the first language of the catalogue. Help is answered before any tool or store starts.
- **Dependencies.** `dependencies sync` installs into the dependencies partition (`node_modules` at the root is a symlink into it). Any `package.json` or `bun.lock` edit makes the installed generation stale: run `dependencies lock --all`, then `dependencies sync --all` once, never an ad-hoc `bun install`. Before it installs, a sync retires every `j/<20 hex>` dependency generation that is neither a current plan generation nor the target of a live binding (progress `dependencies:retire-obsolete:<n>`). On macOS 27 the installer can fail with `EPERM: Operation not permitted: failed to link package`; see Recovery, the sync repairs that case itself. A workspace package created after the last sync (a new `package.json`) cannot be resolved until `dependencies lock --all` and `dependencies sync --all` ran; every route that imports it fails with `Cannot find module` until then (seen on 2026-10-20 with a new framework package, which stopped the `verify` routes and the documentation-law tests). Sync cost: the verification passes were quadratic in the number of bindings. The installed-paths scan now compares a directory with itself when it closes instead of re-reading every ancestor of every entry (progress frames every `scanProgressWorkUnits` = 4,096 work units), the publisher re-checks only the strict ancestors of an endpoint's parent (`javascriptDependencyStrictAncestorsV1`), each published binding gets a 12-`lstat` endpoint fence (`requireJavascriptDependencyEndpointFenceV1`), and the complete fence runs every `fullFenceInterval` = 32 bindings and after the last one (constants in `📚️library/⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/📦️dependencies/💾️storage/🧬️schema/📥️install.json`). Measured on the primary host at load 31 to 52: the scan of one 84,345-entry generation fell from 168 to 302 s to 48 to 64 s, and the last green `dependencies sync --all` (2026-10-20 05:43) took about 31 minutes, where an earlier attempt ran 7,000 s without finishing. Leave the tree alone while a sync runs: a peer edit of a provider file during the walk failed one attempt with `Declared execution physical observation changed`. The suites that pin these fences are the targets `repo:test-javascript-installed-paths`, `repo:test-javascript-endpoint-fence` and `repo:test-javascript-provider-walk` (project `repo`, directory `⚡️caching`). Recorded on 2026-10-20 on the live tree under an issued envelope: the endpoint-fence suite passed (5 tests), the provider-walk suite failed its table test on the row `provider-relinked-in-first-replay` (expected refused; not diagnosed, open), and the installed-paths suite could not load because the installed generation predates the package `@semio-tech/framework-filesystem` that it imports.
- **Dependency generation receipt.** Every `repo` task or graph call first proves that the installed dependencies still match their sources. That proof used to be a complete walk of the workspace (eight directory-tree passes of about 617,000 operations and four provider walks per command, 36 to 58 s per tree pass under load). It is now a sealed receipt per dependency scope, checked cheapest tier first (`Protocol.tiers` in `📚️library/⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/📦️dependencies/🧾️generation/🧬️schema/🔣️.json`): `implementation` (content digest of every file in the runtime import closure of the walk and fence entries, read with `captureBuildSourceClosureV1`; 175 files, 2.1 MB on the measured tree), `runtime` (Bun and Node versions, Bun revision, platform, installer recipe), `producer` (the `.npmrc` and `bunfig.toml` files an installer level could read, and the `BUN_*`, `NPM_CONFIG_*`, proxy and certificate variables, by name and value digest only), `bindings`, `inputs` (manifests, lock, bunfig, patches), `membership` (every walked directory by stamp; a directory younger than 5 s is listed again and compared by projection digest) and `execution` (every physical observation the provider walk made, replayed). Any difference is a typed mismatch (`Protocol.mismatchClasses`) and the command falls back to the complete walk, which then passes or throws exactly as before. Receipts and their evidence are content-addressed files in `.cache/b/d/m/generation`; verification runs under the caller's shared store custody and publishes progress `dependencies:generation-verify:<n>`. Only an `inspect` with exclusive custody and the end of a `dependencies sync` publish a receipt (`publishJavascriptDependencyGenerationReceiptsElseV1`; best effort, a failure prints `[DATA]` and never fails the sync), and a capture defers while any closure file is younger than the process start minus 5 s, so a receipt only vouches for code that ran. Consequence: an edit of any file of the closure (126 of the 175 are process modules) costs the next command one complete walk. Measured read-only on the primary host at load 56 to 65: a complete receipt verifies in 3.6 to 12 s against 36 to 58 s for one tree pass; the closure and producer tiers add about 10 ms. Not yet verified: on 2026-10-20 the generation directory held three zero-byte `*.frontier.ndjson` files and no sealed receipt, so no live run has published and then verified one; Linux, Windows and the devcontainer were not run.
- **Waiting for a store.** A cache image and other shared resources are claimed through a queue (`acquireQueuedResourceLease`, resource `native-fixed-image:<partition path>`): tickets are served in arrival order, a shared ticket joins a cohort only when every earlier ticket is a shared cohort ticket, so a writer is not starved by readers that arrive after it, and a waiter reaps only its immediate predecessor when that process is dead. Waiters poll from 250 ms to at most 1,000 ms (factor 3/2) and sweep at most once per second (`🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🏃️process/🔒️leases/⏳️wait/🧬️schema/📜️policy.json`); with 14 waiters behind one holder for 15 s their CPU time fell from 15.2 s to 2.2 s. A waiter at position 2 or later publishes every 15 s a `lease-queue` progress line, shown in English or German, for example `Waiting for access to native-fixed-image:<path> (shared): position 3 of 15, behind the request of process 12528 (exclusive, queued 8040 s ago)`: the pid names the holder. A line `<command> remains active on dependencies:store-ownership:state:<N> (exclusive, <s> s)` is the generic progress line of a running walk (N counts work), not a wait. `upgradeQueuedResourceLease` turns a held queued shared lease into an exclusive one at its original queue position and consumes the shared hold; asking exclusively for a resource the same process already holds shared still waits for itself until the timeout. The raw `acquireResourceLease` (polling SQLite) is not queued and prefers readers, so mixing it with queued requesters can starve a queued writer; image paths use the queue unless `maximumLeaseDatabaseBytes` is set, and whether the raw primitive should prefer writers is an open owner decision. A single `repo run` that holds an image exclusively for hours (a store ownership walk after a dependency generation change) blocks every other command on that image: read the head ticket before restarting a sync. The target `test-resource-lease-fairness` of `@semio-tech/framework-process` runs the fixture-driven suite (queued Bun and Node clients against a queued writer, Python `sqlite3` readers as the independent client, dead predecessors, CPU cost, progress text, upgrade rules). Recorded: a plain `bun test` of the fairness file on the live tree on 2026-10-20 passed 10 of 11 tests; the failing case (`python-readers-queued-writer`, a timing case against Python readers) passed when run alone, so it is load sensitive. Not yet verified through `repo run`: the first check after the suite was applied (2026-10-20 05:05) is recorded as failed and was not repeated through the runner.
- **Cargo.** Raw `cargo` and `rustc` are refused by the compiler ingress gate in `.cargo/config.toml`. Compile through `repo cargo --store=shared|isolated --configuration=prototype|release [--parallelism=<cores>] -- <cargo subcommand> <args>`; `--store` and `--configuration` are mandatory and the Cargo subcommand follows `--`; without `--manifest-path` Cargo reads the `Cargo.toml` of the working directory (the root workspace when called from the root; `🏃️process/🦀️cargo/🚪️launcher`). Never create a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: the shared store is a fixed-capacity partition and parallel private targets exhaust the disk. Before Cargo starts, `repo cargo` admits the Source of every compilation unit. The store-custody recheck inside that admission is throttled to one per 15 s (`storageRecheckGateV1`; `storageRecheck.intervalMilliseconds` in the admission schema; the first call and the completion call always recheck), and `rustc --print cfg` is computed once per exact argument vector; on a real image at load about 55 an admission unit with 12 reads dropped from 12.3 s to 3.0 s. A compile that finds the Rust image below the compiler charge (4 GiB) frees space through the recovery in `📚️library/⚡️caching/💾️storage/🔒️enforcement/🏗️compiler/📏️capacity/♻️recovery`, which compares the invocation identity of the control (`requireIssuedResourceControlInvocationV1`), not its signal. After a refactor that touches many crates, run small `repo cargo` invocations one after the other, never beside a dependency sync or a graph inference (all three take long store leases); the resumable plan that sequenced the 2026-10-20 recompile window (205 required steps over 644 crates, at most 8 `-p` packages each) is a tool of the coordination ticket, not a `repo` command. Not yet verified: when this was written that window had only started its first lock-refresh step, so the value-family, proctor and activation-closure compiles are still unproven.
- **Zero-touch.** The build CLI must boot on a fresh clone with no `node_modules`, no installed tool and no cache image, because the dependencies live inside a cache partition the CLI itself creates. Everything in the CLI import closure (`📚️library/🏗️build/📜️script.ts`) therefore imports by relative path, never through a bare `@semio-tech/*` specifier, and the `dependencies` command and the environment bootstrap run before the dependencies partition is pinned. After editing a library module, check `repo --help`: it must print the usage line. The same bootstrap must work unchanged in the devcontainer, native Windows, native macOS and native Linux (see Setup per platform below). The law `bun ./📜️script.ts verify zero-touch` keeps that closure honest (see Build owner registry).
- **Cancellation and progress.** The catalogue below declares every operation cancellable (`cancellable`) and every operation except `graph` and `show` as reporting progress (`progress`) in `📚️library/🏗️build/⚙️operations/🔣️catalog.json`; the implementations pass the caller's process control to every path (`runOwnedBuildInLeasesV1` in `📚️library/🏗️build/📜️script.ts`). Not yet verified: no per-operation cancellation run has been recorded.

## 📘️ Build tools guide

This guide is the working view of the tool-neutral build system: which tool to pick, what each operation does, how results are cached and shared, how work is spread over workers and executors, and how to classify a new target. The reference sections after it (Neutral model to Remote cache) hold the contracts, the measurements and the open items. Every command below is an operation of the catalogue `📚️library/🏗️build/⚙️operations/🔣️catalog.json`; `repo <command> --help --lang=en|de` prints the same usage in English or German. Where `repo` is not on the `PATH`, write `bun repo`, the root package script. If several agents or developers share one checkout, start with Many agents in one folder. A German version of this guide follows in the German chapter (Build-Werkzeuge im Alltag).

### 🔀️ Choose the tool

- **Select.** `--tool=nx|bazel` wins, then the environment variable `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL`, then the policy default `nx`. The choice is exported as `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL` to every nested task, so a task that calls `repo` again stays on the tool you chose. The dashboard axis `build-tool` sets the same variable. Anything else is refused ("Unknown build tool").
- **Provision.** `repo tools setup all` installs the pinned Nx and Bazel for this host; `repo tools setup nx` or `repo tools setup bazel` installs one. `workspace:setup` runs the same step.
- **One graph.** Both tools run the same neutral graph with the same task ids (`<project>:<target>[:<configuration>]`). A selection that resolves to different tasks under the two tools is a defect, not a feature.

| | Nx (default) | Bazel (`--tool=bazel`) |
|---|---|---|
| What runs | one `nx run` per selection | one generated workspace per selection; a task is a Bazel action, a selected cached `test` task is a Bazel test, a service is a `bazel run` target |
| Local cache | task results in `nx/artifacts` of the rust partition | action cache of the output base plus the disk cache `bazel/actions` of the products partition |
| Remote cache | Nx self-hosted cache API | Bazel HTTP cache protocol, token through a credential helper |
| Remote execution | no | opt-in for targets that declare `metadata.semio.hermetic` |
| Concurrency | `NX_PARALLEL` | persistent workers and `--jobs` |
| Reuse across selections | by task hash in the task cache | by action key in the disk cache: a second selection reuses the first one's results |

Use Nx for the default flow. Choose Bazel when you want results shared across selections and machines, workers, or remote execution. Both are driven by the same flags; where a tool cannot honour a setting, `repo cache remote status` lists it as `unsupported` (see Remote cache for the Nx fields). Nx is still the default (`defaultTool` in `📚️library/🏗️build/🔣️policy.json`): Bazel is available behind `bun repo` for every operation, and the flip waits for three product runs under Bazel (the architecture course, semio-tech play and the mit-bestand demonstrator). It is one gated change of that policy value; afterwards Nx stays selectable with `--tool=nx` or `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL=nx`. Pin the tool explicitly in scripts and CI (the build workflow already passes `--tool`), so the flip changes nothing for them.

### ⚙️ The neutral operations

| Operation | What it does | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `run` | one task and the tasks it depends on | `repo run @semio-tech/semio-tech-play:serve --tool=bazel` |
| `run-many` | the same targets in many projects (`--projects`, `--exclude`, `tag:`, `name:`, `directory:`, `!` patterns) | `repo run-many --targets=test --projects=tag:rust --tool=bazel` |
| `affected` | targets in the projects changed against `HEAD` (tracked changes plus untracked files) and their dependents | `repo affected --targets=build --base=main --head=HEAD` |
| `exec` | a command once per project, project root as working directory, `SEMIO_PROJECT_NAME` and `SEMIO_PROJECT_ROOT` exported | `repo exec --projects=@semio-tech/repo-client -- bun test` |
| `--dry-run`, `--graph` | print the task graph as a `DryRunPlan` and run nothing; `--graph=<file>` writes it | `repo run-many --targets=build,test --dry-run` |
| `show`, `graph`, `watch` | the neutral graph, and a command rerun on source changes | `repo show projects --json` |
| `cache status --tasks`, `cache clear` | count or empty the selected tool's local task cache | `repo cache status --tasks --tool=bazel` |
| `cache status`, `cache gc` | the cache ledger: every home with budget and use, and lock-aware collection (see Shared caches and budgets) | `repo cache gc --dry-run` |
| `cache remote status`, `cache serve` | the remote cache contract and a local cache server (see Caching model) | `repo cache remote status --tool=bazel` |
| `reset` | invalidate the graph and reset the tool's local state | `repo reset --tool=bazel` |
| `dependencies ensure` | install the locked dependencies only when they are missing or changed; every command that needs them runs it first | `repo dependencies ensure` |
| `capacity status`, `capacity run` | show the host capacity or run any command under a machine-wide grant (see Host capacity) | `repo capacity status` |
| `incremental` | prove in a disposable clone that Nx and Bazel rebuild exactly what an edit affects (see Incremental development) | `repo incremental run --scenario=fw-ts-comment` |
| `inputs` | print what Nx hashes for one task; Bazel records its spawn inputs in the parity evidence instead | `repo inputs @teaching/architecture-course:test` |
| `verify build-parity` | root command: prove that Nx and Bazel produce the same outputs (see Parity verification) | `repo verify build-parity --plans-only` |
| `leases status` | holders, waiters, lock-order violations and deadlock victims of the lease system (see Leases, lock order and queues) | `repo leases status` |
| `concurrency` | prove that simulated agents in one clone never wait for unrelated projects (see Incremental development) | `repo concurrency run --workloads=disjoint-projects` |
| `remote-execution serve`, `status`, `env` | run, inspect or describe the optional local remote execution cluster (see Remote execution) | `repo remote-execution status` |

- **Dry runs and graphs.** `--dry-run` and `--graph` print the same `DryRunPlan` for every tool and start neither Nx nor Bazel: `roots`, `tasks` and `dependencies` (under Bazel also `selected`, and dependencies on services in `continuousDependencies`). The plan says what would run, not what is cached; use `cache status` for that.
- **Affected.** The rule is the same for both tools: the deepest project that owns a changed file, projects whose target inputs name a changed `{workspaceRoot}/…` file, every project when `nx.json` changes, then all dependents. Bazel answers it with a `rdeps` query over a generated package, cross-checked against Nx's own locators on four of five change sets; a change to `bun.lock` alone is the known difference (Nx treats it as "everything", the neutral rule and Bazel see only path ownership). `--projects` is refused for `affected`; narrow with `run-many`.
- **Cancel and progress.** Every operation can be cancelled (Ctrl+C) and reports progress lines while it walks, queues or downloads; a cancelled run stops at the next checkpoint.
- **Bazel specifics.** A selection builds exactly the transitive closure of the selected tasks, nothing else is analysed. `run-many` is one Bazel invocation with all labels, so shared dependencies run once. When a selected task is a test the invocation is `bazel test` (with `--build_tests_only` only when every selected label is a test); `--skip-cache` adds `--nocache_test_results`.

### 🗣️ Language and option spelling

- **No default language.** The CLI never assumes a language for its help, progress and refusal texts. It takes the first source that names an offered language (`en`, `de`): `--lang=<en|de>`, `SEMIO_LANGUAGE`, `LANGUAGE` (first offered entry), the first non-empty of `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES`, `LANG` (`C` and `POSIX` name none), then the operating system language list (macOS preferences, the Windows user locale; other systems state theirs in the environment). `--lang` and `SEMIO_LANGUAGE` must name an offered language; a locale that names an unoffered one is skipped. When nothing resolves, the command refuses before it starts, in English and German, and names every way to choose (for example `SEMIO_LANGUAGE=en` in CI). One module owns the rule: `🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🏃️process/🏗️build/🗣️language`.
- **Keine Standardsprache.** Die CLI nimmt für Hilfe, Fortschritt und Ablehnungen nie eine Sprache an. Maßgeblich ist die erste Quelle, die eine angebotene Sprache (`en`, `de`) nennt: `--lang=<en|de>`, `SEMIO_LANGUAGE`, `LANGUAGE` (erster angebotener Eintrag), die erste nicht leere Variable aus `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES`, `LANG` (`C` und `POSIX` nennen keine), danach die Sprachliste des Betriebssystems. Lässt sich nichts bestimmen, verweigert sich der Befehl vor dem Start auf Englisch und Deutsch und nennt alle Wege zur Auswahl (etwa `SEMIO_LANGUAGE=de` in CI).
- **Options.** An option with a required value (`--tool`, `--workers`, `--lang`, `--configuration`, `--store`, `--output`, `--projects`, ...) may be typed as `--option value` or `--option=value`, also by its short alias; the CLI door rewrites it to `--option=value` before anything else reads it. Options with a separate or optional value and everything after `--` stay as typed. `repo <command> --help` lists, for a subcommand, only the options and examples that belong to it, followed by the notes of its operation.
- **Temporary directories.** `repo incremental` keeps its disposable copy in the folder `sinc` of the temporary directory of the process (inside a test sandbox, the sandbox directory); pass `--root` when that path is longer than 100 characters.

### 💾️ Caching model

A result is reused when the key of the task is unchanged. Each tool derives its key from the same declared `inputs` (files and named groups, environment values, `runtime` command outputs, the closure of the files the command script imports, platform, Bun version, build mode) and from the identity of the build tool itself. Consequences: a file the task reads but does not declare does not invalidate it (a stale hit, not a failure); an edit to the build tool's own implementation re-keys its tasks once (for Bazel the captured implementation is an input of every action), so expect one cold run after pulling such a change; a task that declares the whole repository as input re-keys on any edit and is an owner problem, not a cache problem.

| Layer | Where | Cleared by |
|---|---|---|
| Nx task cache | `.🧬semio/🦑️repo/⚡️cache/rust/fixed/m/nx/artifacts` (rust partition) | `repo cache clear` (`nx reset --onlyCache` inside the bounded Nx namespace) |
| Bazel action cache | output base `bazel/outputs/server/<hash>` of the products partition, one per generated workspace directory; the server idles out after 120 s | `repo cache clear` stops the servers and removes the output bases |
| Bazel disk cache | `.cache/b/p/m/bazel/actions`, collected down to 40 percent of the products budget, entries older than 30 days dropped | `repo cache clear`; survives `bazel clean` and the loss of a server |
| Remote cache | an HTTP endpoint you configure, or `repo cache serve` (store in `.cache/remote-cache`) | the server's own bound and eviction |

All build state below `.cache/b/<partition>` is derived and safe to delete when nothing runs (partitions `d` dependencies, `t` tools, `g` graph, `o` observations, `p` products, `l` leases, `n` Nx pool; see Cache partitions). `--skip-cache` ignores the local task cache of the run (for Bazel it also stops accepting remote hits), `--skip-remote-cache` ignores only the remote cache, and the dashboard axis `cache` offers `use`, `skip-local` and `skip-all`.

- **Early cutoff.** Under Bazel a task whose output snapshot is byte-identical after its input changed does not re-run its dependents (measured: a whitespace-only edit of a generator input re-ran the generator only).
- **Store receipts.** Products that live in a leased store, not in the project directory, declare a receipt as their only output; both tools verify it after every run and re-run that single task uncached when the receipt is damaged. See Store receipts.

**Remote cache.** It is off until `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ENDPOINT` names an `http://` or `https://` origin; the contract and its variables are in the Remote cache section.

```bash
repo cache serve --port 6090
```

```bash
SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:6090 SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ACCESS=read-write repo run @teaching/architecture-course:test --tool=bazel
repo cache remote status --tool=bazel
```

In PowerShell the same is `$env:SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ENDPOINT = "http://127.0.0.1:6090"`, `$env:SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ACCESS = "read-write"`, then `bun repo run @teaching/architecture-course:test --tool=bazel`.

- **Access.** The default is `read-only`; results are pushed only with `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ACCESS=read-write` and `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_UPLOAD` not `false`. The token is read from the variable `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_TOKEN` (or the variable named by `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_TOKEN_ENVIRONMENT`) and never appears in a flag or on disk beyond one build: Bazel receives it through a credential helper, Nx through its own variable. Nx cannot withhold uploads, so for Nx read-only means a read token on the server.
- **`cache remote status`.** Prints the resolved contract (`configured: false` while no endpoint is set), the rendering the selected tool gets with its `unsupported` fields, whether the token variable is set (never its value) and a read-only probe of the endpoint: `reachable`, `authorized` and the HTTP status. It stores nothing.
- **`cache serve`.** Serves Bazel's HTTP cache protocol (`/ac/<sha256>`, `/cas/<sha256>`, blobs verified against their digest) and the Nx self-hosted cache API (`/v1/cache/<hash>`) from this machine until you stop it. Options: `--port` (default 6090, `0` picks a free one), `--host` (default `127.0.0.1`), `--directory`, `--max-bytes` (default 4 GiB, at least 1 MiB; the least recently used entries go first). Tokens come from `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_SERVER_READ_TOKEN` and `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_SERVER_WRITE_TOKEN`; without a token the server refuses to listen beyond loopback. The dashboard tool `build-cache-serve` starts it.
- **Download modes.** `toplevel` (default) and `all`. `minimal` is not part of the contract and the schema refuses it: the Bazel adapter restores the snapshot of every selected task from `bazel-bin`, and with `minimal` the snapshot stays in the remote store, so the restore fails with `ENOENT` and nothing reaches the checkout (measured). Nx always fetches everything.
- **What is shared.** Bazel keys of today's tasks contain the absolute Bun path and the checkout root of the captured implementation, so a hit across machines needs the same user and checkout path. Hermetic actions (next section) have host-independent keys (measured: the same inputs at a different checkout path hit the disk cache).

### 🌍️ Remote execution

Remote execution is optional and opt-in per target. The build system defines the contract and the Bazel rendering; it ships no executor.

- **Declare.** A target is a hermetic action only when its manifest says so: `"metadata": { "semio": { "hermetic": { "version": 1, "tools": ["bun"] } } }` (schema `🏗️build/🏃️execution/🧬️schema/🔒️hermetic.json`). It must also be cached with a declared `outputs` array, run plain `command` strings, have only file inputs (no `runtime` or `env` input, no `{options.…}` or `{env.…}` token), not be continuous and not orchestrate other builds. A target that does not qualify keeps host state; the reason is closed (`undeclared`, `unsupported-execution`, `host-inputs`, `uncached`, `continuous`, `orchestration`, `tool`). A class table (`🏗️build/🏃️execution/🔒️hermetic/🔣️.json`, schema `🏗️build/🏃️execution/🧬️schema/🔒️hermetic-classes.json`) can make a whole target family hermetic instead (`ts-test`, `ts-check`, `ts-build`, `ts-typecheck`, `ts-generate`: 944 of the 3,707 cacheable targets are eligible by static checks); it is rolled out to three canary projects, whose targets run their real scripts inside the action with a declared dependency tree and an issued original authority. No repository target has been run hermetically on the live tree yet, and none remotely, so nothing runs remotely today.
- **What a hermetic action is.** It runs under a pinned Bun that is a declared tool input (downloaded under a lease with progress, verified by size and SHA-256 before extraction), in a sandbox or on a worker, with a strict environment (`LC_ALL=C`, `TZ=UTC`, `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=0`, `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH=0`), no host path in its key, normalised outputs, and it fails on an undeclared write. Hermetic actions run the pinned Bun, not the one on your `PATH`.
- **Switch.** Unset keeps every action local; a malformed value fails closed.

| Variable | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `SEMIO_REMOTE_EXECUTION_ENDPOINT` | `grpc://`, `grpcs://` or `unix:` URI of the executor | none (local) |
| `SEMIO_REMOTE_EXECUTION_PLATFORM` | worker platform: `darwin`, `linux` or `windows` plus `-arm64` or `-x64` | `linux-x64` |
| `SEMIO_REMOTE_EXECUTION_PROPERTIES` | extra worker properties, `name=value,…` (up to 16), added to the platform's `OSFamily` and `Arch` | none |
| `SEMIO_REMOTE_EXECUTION_JOBS` | concurrent actions while the executor is on | 128 (at most 512) |

- **Routing.** Only hermetic actions go to the executor, with `--noremote_local_fallback`: an unreachable executor fails the build, it never runs the action locally. Host-state actions stay local (`no-remote-exec`).
- **Executor requirements.** A service for Bazel's Remote Execution API v2 with content store, action cache and scheduler; workers whose properties match the platform (Buildbarn matches exactly; BuildBuddy uses `container-image` and `pool`); Linux workers need only the uploaded Bun, no checkout. Execution flags never carry cache settings: the cache endpoint, instance, upload, download mode and token stay with the remote cache contract, whose endpoint is an `http(s)` origin. Pointing Bazel at one gRPC service for cache and execution is not defined by the contract yet.
- **Platforms.** Network isolation of a hermetic action is enforced on Linux and on remote workers; macOS' sandbox cannot block it (measured with Bazel 8.4.2). On Windows there is no sandbox, so the runner refuses a hermetic action there (the source link is a junction); run Windows hermetic targets remotely.
- **Optional local cluster.** `repo remote-execution serve` runs a local Buildbarn cluster (storage with content store and action cache, frontend, scheduler, worker, runner) in containers until you stop it and then removes the containers and volumes. It needs a running container runtime with the compose plugin; every image is an official one, pinned by digest. The frontend answers on `127.0.0.1:8980` and its health route on `8981` (`--port` and `--diagnostics-port` move them); the cluster has no authentication and listens on loopback only. `--platform=<id>` selects the worker platform (default: the one that matches this machine's CPU, `linux-arm64` or `linux-x64`). `repo remote-execution status` shows the runtime, the containers and whether the frontend answers, and `repo remote-execution env --platform=linux-x64` prints the variables a client sets, one `NAME=value` per line. The runner has no network, so a hermetic action there gets a real network namespace without a network. It is a developer service, never a dependency: with `SEMIO_REMOTE_EXECUTION_ENDPOINT` unset every action stays local.

### 👷️ Worker pools

Persistent workers keep a process alive between actions, so an action no longer pays for starting and loading the build tool again.

- **Switch.** `--workers on|off`, then `SEMIO_BUILD_WORKERS`, then the worker policy in `📚️library/🏗️build/🔣️policy.json` (`enabled`, on by default). `--parallel <n>` or `SEMIO_BUILD_PARALLEL` sets the number of concurrent tasks (default: the number of cores, at most 8).
- **Kinds.** The kind is derived from the target name and continuity, never from the tool; the first matching rule wins.

| Kind | Target names | Instances at most |
|---|---|---|
| `service` | `continuous: true` | none (never a worker) |
| `test` | `test`, `smoke`, `mutation`, `bench`, `e2e` (also `<name>-…`) | 8 |
| `check` | `check`, `lint`, `verify`, `typecheck`, `format-check`, `contract`, `schema`, `stdio` (also `<name>-…`), and names ending in `-check` | 8 |
| `generate` | names with `generate` or `generator` | 4 |
| `build` | `build`, `prepare`, `activate`, `wasm`, `native`, `package`, `bundle`, `cpp`, `publish`, `canonical`, `preview`, `setup`, `deps` (also `<name>-…`) | 2 |
| `script` | everything else | 4 |

- **Budget.** All pools together may use 30 percent of the host's memory at 512 MB per worker. Each kind gets one instance first (test, check, generate, build, script, as far as memory holds), the remaining instances go to the kinds in the same order up to their bound and the job count; a kind that finds none runs without workers.
- **Bazel.** Workers speak Bazel's JSON worker protocol, run the action body inside the worker process (leaf commands stay separate process trees), support cancellation and shrink the pool under memory pressure. Worker and non-worker actions have the same action key, so caches are shared across both modes. Actions that never use workers: services, hermetic actions and tests run as `bazel test`. Cargo tasks run one at a time.
- **Nx.** Nx forks a process per task, so only `NX_PARALLEL` is mapped (to the job count); `NX_BATCH_MODE` is set only for an executor that registers a batch implementation, which none does. Every other worker setting (pools, instance bounds, memory, sandboxing) is unsupported under Nx.
- **Measured.** CPU per action fell from 2.50 s to 1.46 s in steady state; wall clock was not consistently better on a saturated host, because 85 percent of a warm in-worker request is opening the graph cache store.
- **Turn them off** with `--workers off` to compare against one process per action, to debug a single task, or when memory is scarce.

### 🚦️ Task dispositions

Every task has a tool family (`bun-script`, `tsc`, `vitest`, `bun-test`, `vite`, `playwright`, `go`, `python`, `dotnet`, `latex`, `wasm`, `cargo`, `docker`, `aggregate`) and one disposition. Nx runs all of them as `nx run`; Bazel maps them as follows (`📚️library/🏗️build/🏃️execution/🧬️schema/🎯️coverage.json`).

| Disposition | Meaning | Under Bazel |
|---|---|---|
| `action` | cached, replayed from the action cache | cached build action, output snapshot restored into the checkout |
| `test` | cached and named `test` or `test-…`, `outputs: []`, nothing else selected consumes it, at most 10,000 declared files | `bazel test`: the passed result is cached, `(cached) PASSED` on the next run; not used where the platform has no runfiles (Windows) |
| `interactive` | `continuous`, runs until stopped | `bazel run` target; it starts on every run and is never produced |
| `uncached` | always executes; its recorded reason says why | action tagged `no-cache` |
| `refused` | `cache` or `outputs` left undeclared (reasons `undeclared`, `unbounded`) | fail-closed until the owner declares what is missing |

Static census of 2026-10-20 (8,028 tasks of the reconstructed graph, with the classification sets applied): 3,771 cached (actions and tests), 1,320 interactive, 2,937 uncached with a recorded class, 0 refused and 0 uncached for the reason `declared` (373 before).

### 🏷️ Classify a new target

Answer these in order; the first yes decides.

1. **Does it run until stopped** (server, watcher, shell)? `"continuous": true`, class `continuous`, reason `serves-until-stopped`. It becomes `interactive`.
2. **Does it read only repository files, write only declared outputs (or none), use no network, host store, lease, clock or running process?** Then cache it: `"cache": true`, `inputs` for everything it reads outside its project, `outputs` (`[]` for a pure check), and give it a cached name family or its own decision (below).
3. **Otherwise** `"cache": false` plus the decision that names the reason.

| Class | Reasons | Use for |
|---|---|---|
| `deterministic` | `pure-function-of-inputs` (declare outputs), `check-without-outputs` | cached checks, tests, generators with declared outputs |
| `continuous` | `serves-until-stopped` | servers, watchers, shells |
| `live` | `drives-running-process`, `exercises-host-resources` | e2e against a running process, tests of real locks, storage, process trees |
| `side-effecting` | `mutates-workspace`, `mutates-host-store`, `publishes-remote`, `publishes-live-state` | formatters, scaffolders, installers, releases |
| `diagnostic` | `measures-environment`, `reads-caller-input`, `admission-refusal` | benchmarks, doctors, tools driven by their arguments |
| `aggregate` | `orders-prerequisites`, `reenters-build-tool` | pure ordering; a verb that runs other targets through `repo` should declare them in `dependsOn` instead |
| `managed-output`, `driver-owned` | `outputs-in-managed-store`, `outputs-are-store-links`, `freshness-owned-by-driver` | store-published builds and driver-owned playground steps |

The policy resolves a target name in this order (`🎯️target-policy`): continuous names, `cache: false`, the policy's uncached names, mutating and live names, then your decision in `metadata.semio.cache`, then the policy's `targetClasses` (name families such as `test`, `check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `verify`, `build`, `generate`), then a `verify` command. **A target that none of them classifies never replays** (`unclassified`, `no-cache-classification`), and `"cache": true` alone does not change that, so give an unusual name its decision:

```json
"digest-inputs": {
  "executor": "repo:script",
  "cache": true,
  "inputs": ["default", "^default"],
  "outputs": [],
  "options": { "command": "bun ./📜️script.ts digest-inputs" },
  "metadata": { "semio": { "cache": { "version": 1, "class": "deterministic", "reason": "check-without-outputs" } } }
}
```

```json
"measure-startup": {
  "executor": "repo:script",
  "cache": false,
  "outputs": [],
  "options": { "command": "bun ./📜️script.ts measure-startup" },
  "metadata": { "semio": { "cache": { "version": 1, "class": "diagnostic", "reason": "measures-environment" } } }
}
```

- **Outputs.** A cached target must produce every declared output that is not a glob: otherwise it fails with "Declared output of a replayed Bazel task was not produced". Set `metadata.semio.optionalOutputs: true` only for an output that is legitimately absent. A `pure-function-of-inputs` target without declared outputs is demoted to `side-effecting`.
- **Test under Bazel.** Name it `test` or `test-<what>`, keep it cached with `outputs: []` and under 10,000 declared files (every declared file is linked into the test's runfiles before every run, even on a hit).
- **Remote execution.** Add the `hermetic` declaration (see Remote execution) only when the target truly reads its declared inputs alone.
- **Law.** The cache-correctness law (`📚️library/⚖️laws/🔒️cache-correctness`) reads every manifest through the production policy and fails for an unclassified target, a cached target that the policy overrides, an uncached target without a recorded reason, empty inputs, a producer without outputs, an unhashed environment override, a named input nobody declares, outputs that escape or overlap, and a mutating or network command in a cached target.

### 🔁️ Incremental builds and how to verify them

An incremental build re-runs exactly the tasks whose declared inputs changed and replays the rest. Prove it with the operations you already have; the CI job `smoke` is written to do the same for both tools on `@teaching/architecture-course:test` (the workflow has not run yet). `repo incremental` repeats this proof in a disposable clone for both tools side by side and judges the executed tasks against the declared inputs (Incremental development).

```bash
repo cache clear --tool=bazel
repo cache status --tool=bazel
repo run @teaching/architecture-course:test --tool=bazel
repo cache status --tool=bazel
repo run @teaching/architecture-course:test --tool=bazel
repo cache status --tool=bazel
```

1. Note the counts after `cache clear`; after the first run `entries` and `bytes` grew and, for Bazel, `activity.executed` counts the actions that ran.
2. The second run spawns no work: Bazel's summary shows no `local`, `worker` or sandbox spawn (or `disk cache hit`), `entries` do not grow and `activity.executed` stays, while `activity.cacheHits` counts the hits.
3. Edit a file the task declares: only that task and its dependents run (a dependent whose input snapshot is unchanged is replayed). Edit a file it does not declare and nothing runs: the input list is too short.
4. `repo cache clear` followed by the run proves a cold miss; `--skip-cache` forces execution once without clearing; `--dry-run` shows the plan the run would execute.
5. Expect extra work after: editing the build tool's own files (every action is re-keyed once), switching `prototype` and `release` (the mode is part of every key), and editing a declared input while the build runs (Bazel prints `Skipping uploading outputs because of concurrent modifications` and the next run executes again).

### 👥️ Many agents in one folder

Several developers and agents work in one checkout at the same time, on different crates and packages. The build system is arranged so that unrelated work does not wait: results are content addressed and shared, a writer holds a shared resource only while it publishes, per-session state is private, and every wait is a first-in-first-out queue that names who holds the resource. This section is the map; the sections after it hold the details. Every feature carries its state. **Live** means applied in the checkout. **Staged** means built, tested in an overlay copy of the tree and waiting to be applied: its commands do not exist yet, and its description is the contract it ships with (the numbers quoted for it are overlay measurements, not live ones).

| Feature | Command or switch | State |
|---|---|---|
| Host capacity governor | `repo capacity status`, `repo capacity run`, `SEMIO_HOST_CAPACITY=off` | live |
| Capacity ramp and bounded overtaking | automatic | live |
| Fair lease queues that name the holder | the `lease-queue` progress line | live |
| Lock order, deadlock detection, lease status | `repo leases status`, `SEMIO_LEASE_ORDER` | live |
| Incremental conformance judge | `repo incremental` | live |
| Concurrent-session acceptance harness | `repo concurrency` | live |
| Cache ledger, budgets, lock-aware collection | `repo cache status`, `repo cache gc` | live |
| Graph policy and graph generations | `--graph-policy=fresh\|current` | live |
| Per-session Bazel server slots | `SEMIO_BAZEL_SERVERS`, `SEMIO_BAZEL_SERVICES` | live |
| Cargo lanes and compile-unit cache | `repo cargo --store=shared`, `SEMIO_CARGO_LANE`, `SEMIO_UNIT_CACHE_SLOTS` | live |
| Shared Cargo build-script runs and component passes | `SEMIO_UNIT_CACHE_BUILD_SCRIPTS` | live |
| Nx graph reuse and pool without hold-and-wait | automatic | live |
| Nx daemon cap and idle shutdown | `SEMIO_NX_DAEMONS`, `SEMIO_NX_DAEMON_IDLE_MS` | live |
| Nx cache put slots | `SEMIO_NX_PUT_SLOTS` | live |
| Concurrent dependency generations | `repo dependencies ensure`, `repo dependencies sync` | live |
| Lease-free generation maintenance and size admission | automatic | staged |
| Incremental dependency sync | `repo dependencies sync` | live |
| Per-crate cache keys | automatic | live |
| Narrow, derived task inputs | automatic | live |
| Per-package TypeScript check units | library engine, no command yet | staged |
| Test sandbox | automatic in `repo run <project>:test` | live |
| Output parity of Nx and Bazel | `repo verify build-parity` | live |
| Recovery after a reboot or a crash | automatic at the next `repo` command | live |
| CI on the neutral interface with a shared remote cache | `BUILD_TOOL`, `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ENDPOINT` | staged |

Rules for working together:

1. **Everything through `repo`.** Never run `nx`, `bazel`, `cargo` or `rustc` yourself and never set a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: the shared stores are fixed-capacity partitions, and private copies exhaust the disk (a fleet of private Cargo targets reached 926 GB on 2026-10-20).
2. **Work on your own project.** Edit the crate or package you own and run its targets; `repo run <project>:<target>` builds the closure of that task only. Files that many projects read are shared inputs and re-key wide areas: the root `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`, `nx.json`, the `tsconfig` roots, every `package.json` and `bun.lock`, the taxonomy files. Batch such edits and tell the others (Incremental development shows what they cost).
3. **Ask the machine before a heavy command.** Wrap an ad-hoc heavy command in `repo capacity run --memory=<megabytes> -- <command>` and read `repo capacity status` before you start several things at once (Host capacity).
4. **Waiting is a queue, not a hang.** Read the line it prints; it names the holder. A holder that makes progress works for everybody: do not kill a process you did not start. Ctrl+C cancels yours and gives back what it held.
5. **One dependency change at a time.** Lock and sync are repository-wide. Run them once, after the last manifest edit; everybody else runs `repo dependencies ensure`, which installs only when something is missing or changed.
6. **Tests use their sandbox.** A test never writes into the checkout, into a shared cache route or to a fixed port (Test sandbox).
7. **Leave build state alone.** Never delete or detach anything below `.cache/b`, never move a receipt; use the controls named in Recovery after a reboot or a crash.

### 🧱️ Incremental development

The aim is that an edit costs the work that depends on it and nothing else, for every agent at the same time. The table says per layer what the unit of reuse is and what an edit re-does.

| Layer | Unit of reuse | An edit re-does | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| Task key | the declared inputs of one task | the tasks whose declared inputs contain the file, then the dependents whose input snapshot changed (early cutoff under Bazel) | live |
| Derived inputs | an input set derived by rule instead of a whole-repository glob | tasks that read broadly declare what they read: the tasks above 100,000 declared files fell from 243 to 11, and the 947 tasks that changed declare 12.0 M instead of 34.3 M input files (static reconstruction of 8,028 tasks) | live |
| Rust key | the resolution identity of one crate: its locked dependency slice, the workspace fields it inherits, toolchain and config | the crates whose slice or inherited field changed; comments, formatting and workspace member lists move nothing | live |
| Project graph | one immutable fragment per owner inside a generation | the dirty owners and the owners that consumed the same fact; readers take no lease | live |
| Dependencies | one generation per scope (one `bun.lock` and its `node_modules`) | the scopes whose generation key moved; the rest is verified by receipt | live |
| TypeScript | one program and one incremental state per owner, compiler and configuration; staged: one check unit per package, cut by a declaration boundary | any edit re-checks the whole program; staged: a body edit re-checks one unit (2.9 s measured against 27 s), a signature edit re-checks its dependents until their declarations are equal | staged |
| Rust compile | one compile unit, content addressed, in a cache shared by all agents | the units whose source or dependency artifacts changed; the focused `-p` crate keeps rustc's incremental state in the agent's own lane | live |
| Bazel | action key in the shared disk cache | the actions whose key moved; dependents are spared when an output is byte-identical | live |
| Nx | task hash in the task cache | the tasks whose hash moved | live |

**Prove it with `repo incremental`** (live). The command judges, in a disposable clone of the checkout, that Nx and Bazel rebuild exactly what an edit affects, with the same scenarios under both tools and the results side by side. `prepare` clones the tracked and untracked files into `--root` (default `/tmp/sinc`, `<drive>:\sinc` on Windows; refused when the root lies inside the checkout or contains it) and provisions the clone with its own `preflight`, `tools setup all` and `dependencies sync --all`; every edit of a scenario happens inside the clone, never in your tree.

```bash
repo incremental list
repo incremental plan --scenario=fw-ts-comment
repo incremental prepare
repo incremental run --scenario=fw-ts-comment --implementations=nx,bazel
repo incremental discard
```

- **Scenarios.** 17 scenarios in 7 areas (framework TypeScript, Rust crate, wasm component, Vite app, test target, lockfile, product activation) and 6 edit kinds (comment-only, body change, signature change, config, lockfile, unrelated sibling). Per implementation a scenario runs prime, `warm` (no edit: only uncached tasks may execute), `edit` (one line appended to one source) and `revert` (the exact original text is restored, also after a failure or a cancellation, and everything must come from the cache). `--phases=warm,edit,revert` narrows the phases.
- **Verdicts.** The expected set is computed from the declared inputs: tasks that read the file directly must run, dependents whose output changed must run, dependents whose output is provably identical may be spared, uncached tasks always run. `pass`; `stale` (a task that had to run did not); `excess` (a task ran that may not); `stale-excess`; `failed`; `skipped`. Bazel follows every dependency and cuts off on output digests, Nx follows only tasks that consume outputs: that is the one place the tools legitimately differ, and the report lists it as `only nx:` and `only bazel:`.
- **Observation and output.** Bazel is read from its compact execution log, Nx from its own run record. The report is English or German (`--lang`), `--json` prints JSON, `--file=<report>` writes it; progress is published per stage, every stage is a cancellation checkpoint, and the exit code fails when any run is not a pass.

**Prove concurrency with `repo concurrency`** (live). The command is the acceptance test for everything in this chapter that claims "no waiting": N simulated agents work in the same disposable clone (the one `repo incremental prepare` provisions), edit different projects at the same time and run build, test and check through both tools; then the same sessions run once more in a row as the serial reference.

```bash
repo concurrency list
repo concurrency plan --workloads=all
repo concurrency run --workloads=disjoint-projects --sessions=3 --implementations=nx,bazel
```

- **Workloads.** `disjoint-projects` (a TypeScript, a Rust, a Go and a schema project, edited and built by one agent each), `same-target-cold` (one stale-making edit, three agents build the same target, started 250 ms apart), `warm-fanout` (primed, three agents build the same target) and `readers-while-edit` (an editor lists projects after a source edit while two readers do the same). `--sessions=<n>` takes the first n agents (default 3, at most 8). `plan` refuses a workload in which an agent edits a file that another agent's targets declare as an input.
- **Criteria** (`--criteria=default|warm|smoke` replaces a workload's own set). `default`: every step succeeds, results equal the serial run (exit code, declared output digests, stdout digest), no wait above 5 s for an unrelated project in any lock class (graph facts, dependency image, Bazel server, Cargo units, capacity admission, toolchain acquisition, other), and a task executes in one session only while the others execute no action for it. `warm` demands zero executed actions; `smoke` demands success and serial equality only. The report prints one verdict per owning executor: pass, fail, or unproven when only skipped rules remain.
- **What a wait measurement can prove.** Bazel's `Another command (pid=N) is running` and Cargo's `Blocking waiting for file lock` lines give exact waits. A lease publishes its progress row only every 15 s, so lease waits are a lower bound: a bound above the limit still fails a criterion, a bound below it leaves the criterion unproven. Exact lease waits need `SEMIO_LEASE_TRACE=<file>`, which the lease module writes for acquisitions that waited 100 ms or more (an optional part of the harness set). Nx writes one run record per cache directory, so concurrent Nx runs are matched by time window and marked `ambiguous` when they overlap.
- **Not a speed-up test.** The serial reference runs after the concurrent phase and is therefore warm: the harness proves equal results and absent waits, not a makespan.

**What an edit costs (Rust).** Static model over the repository: 240 Cargo workspaces, 3,374 crates and 12,134 inferred or authored cached targets.

| Edit | Crates and targets re-keyed today | With per-crate keys (live) |
|---|---|---|
| comment or formatting in the root `Cargo.toml`, `.cargo/config.toml` or `rust-toolchain.toml`; a crate appended to the root `[workspace].members` | 3,374 and 12,134 | 0 and 0 |
| a root workspace dependency that one crate uses | 3,374 and 12,134 | 1 and 3 |
| a `Cargo.lock` package that one crate uses | 139 and 827 (Nx), 564 and 2,964 (Bazel) | 1 and 5 |
| `[build].rustflags` or the toolchain channel | 3,374 and 12,134 | 3,374 and 12,134 (correct) |
| an edit of a nested workspace manifest (`🧰️framework/Cargo.toml`: lints, dependency entries) | 0 and 0 (a stale hit) | 2,274 to 3,258 crates (a correctness fix) |
| a source edit in a leaf, median, 90th-percentile or hub crate | 1, 21, 2,233 and 3,080 crates | unchanged: the fan-in is intrinsic |

The fan-in is a property of the dependency graph. The corrected census (resolving workspace dependencies through the owning workspace) shows a mean reach of 363 crates (11.3 percent), a longest chain of 157 and 354 crates that reach 2,000 or more; an edit in the eight bottom crates of the value tower rebuilds about 2,915 of 3,213 crates because of the orphan rule. Splits of the hottest crates are planned, not applied.

**Faster loops.**

- Select narrowly: `repo run <project>:<target>` builds the closure of one task; check the plan first with `--dry-run`.
- For Rust use `repo cargo --store=shared --configuration=prototype -- check -p <crate> --lib`: `check` is cheaper than `build`, and switching `prototype` and `release` re-keys everything.
- Stay on one tool and one mode while you iterate: each tool has its own cache, and the mode is part of every key.
- Do not edit the build tool's own files in a loop: every action is re-keyed once per edit.
- TypeScript units are a library engine; no `repo` command runs them yet, and the monolithic `typecheck` stays the release gate: on the measured program the unit-wise check reported 37 of 43 diagnostics, none extra, so it is a development gate until the six monolithic-only diagnostics are understood. Declaration emit must be able to name every exported type; the findings (a `brand` member, function-local classes with `private` members, inferred types that name `node_modules` paths) are owner fixes listed with the engine.

### 🕸️ Project graph and graph policy

`repo show projects`, `repo graph` and every task command start from the neutral project graph, which is a chain of immutable generations.

- **Live today.** A reader whose seal is current is served by a shared reader that takes no facts writer lease and never queues behind a builder. A missing or stale seal sends the command to the single exclusive builder, which holds `repository-graph-facts:<workspace>` for the whole rebuild; everybody who needs a fresh graph queues behind it, including agents that did not touch the edited project (on 2026-10-20 one `repo exec --dry-run` held that lease for 2.5 hours while three other commands waited). Publication itself had to change too: the expanded graph is about 859 MB of JSON, above the 512 MiB publication limit, so the graph is published compactly (closure inventories stay references that every reader expands, about 431 MB), pack descriptors are bounded (a least-recently-used set of 32), the owner lookup of each file walks up its path instead of scanning 3,656 projects, and every build prints per-phase counters and one `[graph-phase]` summary line (`SEMIO_GRAPH_PHASES=1` or `SEMIO_BUILD_PROFILE_INPUTS=1` print every row).
- **Generations.** Every build publishes an immutable generation: one content-addressed fragment per owner (its neutral node, its outgoing edges, its module facts) and one fact index in which every distinct observation row is stored once. The pointer `current.json` flips by a single compare-and-swap after all documents are written, so a reader always sees one complete generation and takes no lease. An edit dirties its own owner and the owners that consumed the same fact (a membership proof from directory stamps replaces the tree walk); only the dirty owners are inferred, in one call, so the shared import closure is walked once per build. Writers coalesce on one builder custody, and the holder returns a peer's generation when the work is already done. The facts lease is held only for the pointer swap (a test holds a 300 ms build for at least 290 ms under the build lease and under 100 ms under the facts lease). `repo show project <name>` expands only the closure of that project.
- **Policy.** `--graph-policy=fresh|current` (or `SEMIO_BUILD_GRAPH_POLICY`) applies to `graph` and `show`; task commands (`run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `exec`, `watch`) always use `fresh`.

| Policy | Serves | Waits |
|---|---|---|
| `fresh` (default) | a generation that verifies against the tree; the dirty owners are rebuilt first when something it needs is dirty | only for the builder, and only when something is dirty |
| `current` | the newest sealed generation at once, possibly older than your last edit | never |

- **Oracle.** `SEMIO_BUILD_GRAPH_VERIFY=1` runs the unchanged full inference once more and names the first project whose node, edges, external nodes or named inputs differ. Use it after changing graph code.
- **Applying the generations.** The change invalidated every graph receipt and every project record once; the next graph build after it was cold and `repo tools setup nx` ran again afterwards.
- **A cold graph is the slowest command.** On 2026-10-20 the all-workspace inference of 3,735 projects ran at 6 projects per minute at host load 39 and at 33 per minute at load 21 to 27; it is bound by garbage collection of a 7 to 8 GB heap, not by leases or disk, so keep the host load below 25 while it runs. A pass publishes the snapshot it saw: a project that raced an edit is published and marked stale for the next generation, never restarted. Files that are not code (a JSON catalogue, a label, a schema) are recorded by shape and code files by their import analysis, so a body edit of a closure file no longer stales the roughly 670 native records (an import change still does); the closure revision is the digest of the path list; and the rows that thousands of records repeat are stored once as shared content-defined blocks (`np/<24 hex>.json`). The persisted per-project records survive a restart, but a restart repeats the pre-project phases (5 to 8 minutes) and every waiting command with it.

### 🗃️ Shared caches and budgets

Caches are shared where sharing is safe and private where a second writer would corrupt or serialize the first. Every shared cache is content addressed (a key is the hash of its inputs, an entry is written to a temporary name and renamed into place), so two agents that compute the same key at the same time at worst compute it twice.

| Cache | Shared by | Bounded by | Cleared or collected by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dependency generations (`.cache/b/d`, `j/<key>`) | every command; a generation is immutable once its ready marker exists | partition `d`, 8 GiB; a sync retires generations that no plan and no binding names | `repo dependencies sync`; the retention rule below |
| Nx task cache (`nx/artifacts` in the rust partition) | all agents (concurrent writers of one hash are safe) | 3 GiB, declared in `nx.json` as `maxCacheSize` | `repo cache clear` |
| Bazel disk cache (`.cache/b/p/m/bazel/actions`) | all selections and all sessions | 40 percent of the products budget; entries older than 30 days dropped | `repo cache clear` |
| Bazel output bases | one per session slot (`s0`, `s1`, ...; a slot has one holder) | at most 6 build slots and 4 service slots, 6 generated packages per slot, 7 days | `repo cache clear` and the collector of slots |
| Cargo build state | a private lane per agent (`m/u/l/<id>`) plus one content-addressed unit cache (`m/u`) for all | the rust partition, 16 GiB | idle lanes after 24 hours, then by recency when less than 10 percent is free |
| Project graph (`.cache/b/g`) | every command | partition `g`, 4 GiB | `repo reset` |
| Test sandboxes | none (one per run, below the OS temporary directory) | removed on disposal; dead owners are reclaimed | the next sandbox creation |
| Remote cache | other machines and users | the server's own bound | the server |

The partitions and their capacities are described in Cache partitions.

**Cache ledger** (live). Every cache byte has exactly one home: a fixed partition, a nested area inside a partition with its own budget (`nx-artifacts` 3 GiB, `cargo-store` the remainder of the rust partition, `bazel-actions` 40 percent of products), or a budgeted area of a declared loose root (`repository` capped at 20 GiB, `native` at 256 MiB). Anything else is `retired` (a one-time rule in the ledger, removed after its quiet time; dead installs of old tools, the old lease, closure and preparation roots) or `undeclared` (no declared writer; collected after 14 days without use, native after 7). The runtime door `repoCacheDirectory` refuses an undeclared first segment with an English or German message, and a law scans the tracked sources for `⚡️cache` literals, so a new cache needs a ledger entry first.

```bash
repo cache status
repo cache gc --dry-run
repo cache gc --area=<id>,<id>
repo cache status --tasks --tool=bazel
```

- **`cache status`** prints the ledger: each home, its budget, its use (`at least` when the entry cap of the walk was hit) and what is collectable; it takes no lease, so it works while the dependency image is held. `--json` prints JSON and `--lang=en|de` selects the language.
- **`cache gc` collects by default.** Always run `--dry-run` first and read the list: it proposes whole dead installs below `.🧬semio/🦑️repo/⚡️cache/tools` (about 25 GiB) and age-eligible undeclared directories, and you must check that none of them is a live cache of a product.
- **Lock-aware.** Collection re-measures every unit right before removing it and never removes a unit that is a lease database whose exclusive transaction cannot be taken without waiting, that sits in a queue with a ticket of a living process, that lies in an area whose declared lease cannot be taken immediately, that was touched within the area's quiet time or that changed after the scan. Hard links count once and links are never followed. Removal is batched, cancellable (exit code 130) and reports progress. Areas that their own tool manages (Nx, Cargo, Bazel, the dashboard) are reported, not collected; test evidence is delegated to the same pruning call as `cache-prune`.
- **Not verified.** A live run, the `statfs` path of a mounted image (tests cover detached, directory and absent states), Linux, Windows, and Nx honouring `maxCacheSize` at run time.

**Dependency generations** (live). A writer runs in phases and holds only what each phase needs. `build` installs fresh scopes into new generations under a shared image lease plus one builder lease; `publish` switches the bindings and replaces the per-scope pointer atomically (the only window in which readers are refused, 400 ms in the test, waiting at most 15 minutes for readers to drain); `collect` removes unreferenced generations through a tombstone rename. Readers hold the bindings gate and a pin on the generation they read. A second `ensure` that finds the builder busy rechecks readiness every 5 s and returns as soon as the build made it unnecessary; four concurrent ensures start one build. Retention: an unreferenced generation is kept 6 hours after it was retired, the newest retired generation of a scope 24 hours (rollback target), both shrink to 0 while the image has less than 25 percent free, at most 16 are removed per run and a pinned generation never. One manifest edit builds one scope. Maintenance and admission (staged): collection holds only the shared image, with no build lease, no bindings gate and no whole-image hold, so a running build never blocks it; a generation goes only if no pointer's current one, no live binding and no pin holds it and the retention rule is due (pin, tombstone rename, delete), and the builder pins every plan generation before it creates or resumes it, which is what makes lease-free collection safe. Before every fresh scope the expected size (the size recorded in that scope's pointer, else the largest recorded, else 1 GiB, times 1.15) must fit the free bytes; if it does not, collection reclaims at once (with the pressure grace, which also frees rollback targets), the size is measured again, and if it still does not fit the sync refuses before any install with an English or German diagnostic (`admissionRefused`); published generations stay untouched and the next `ensure` resumes with the scopes already built. Until then a scope larger than the free space fails with `ENOSPC` without touching readers. Open: writing under shared custody has not run on a real image, whole-image maintenance that needs quiescence (reset, layout) still waits for a build, and there is no command yet to collect dependency generations by hand.

**In CI** (staged). The following describes the staged workflow. `.github/workflows/build-system.yml` is built around one value, `BUILD_TOOL` (default `bazel`; the dispatch input `tool` switches to `nx`), and every build step is a `bun repo` call that takes the tool from `--tool="$BUILD_TOOL"`: no step names an implementation, an installer, a flag or a cache path. The jobs are `plan` (the `affected` range of the pull request, push or dispatch), `smoke` (Linux, macOS and Windows: zero-touch start, `tools setup`, `dependencies sync --all` and a cache proof that stores once and serves the second run with the remote cache skipped), `affected` (builds and tests only the changed projects and their dependents, reports the hit rate and uploads the JUnit XML of every executed test) and `parity` (both tools select the same tasks for the range). The shared remote cache is configured only by repository settings: the variable `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_ENDPOINT` (an `http(s)` origin of `repo cache serve` or any Bazel HTTP cache that also speaks the Nx cache API), the optional variable `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_INSTANCE` and the secrets `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_READ_TOKEN` and `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_WRITE_TOKEN`; pull requests, forks and dispatches get read-only access without upload and the read token, only a push to the default branch gets `read-write` with upload and the write token. Without an endpoint the local task cache directory travels through the GitHub cache (key `disk-<tool>-<os>-<arch>-<sha>`, restored always, saved only by default-branch pushes). After every Bazel `test` step the adapter exports the `test.xml` of each label to the directory named by `SEMIO_BUILD_TEST_RESULTS_DIR`. The workflow has not run yet; likely first failures are `preflight` and `tools setup` on the Linux and Windows runners, and a core change selects almost every project (hours on a cold first run).

**Across machines.** The remote cache is the cross-machine layer (see Caching model and Remote cache). Hermetic actions have host-independent keys; the keys of today's host-state tasks contain the Bun path and the checkout root, so a hit across machines needs the same user and checkout path. The Cargo unit cache and the TypeScript declaration store are local to a machine; a remote layer for them is not defined.

### 🌡️ Host capacity

Nothing used to know what the machine could carry: every command started at once and each tool sized itself to the whole host, which ended in watchdog kernel panics on 2026-09-28, 2026-09-29 and 2026-10-20 under load. The host capacity governor is one admission domain per user: every heavy `repo` command (`run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `exec`, `watch`, `cargo`, `show`, `graph`, `dependencies`) is admitted before it starts, waits in a first-come-first-served queue (interactive before batch) when the host cannot carry it, and gets a grant that sets the parallelism of the selected tool. Light commands pass untouched. State: live.

```bash
repo capacity status
repo capacity run --memory=6144 -- bun test ./some.test.ts
repo capacity run --operation=dependencies -- bun install
```

- **`capacity status`** prints the host sample (available memory, the floor, spare, pressure, swap, load, open files, attached images), every running grant with what it reserved and what it really holds, and every waiting request; `--json` prints JSON.
- **`capacity run`** puts any other heavy command (a test runner, an installer) through the same queue: `--memory=<megabytes>` reserves exactly that, `--operation=<name>` reserves the estimate of a catalogue operation, `--cpu=<cores>` and `--jobs=<n>` size the request. The command gets the grant in its environment and the grant is returned when it ends, even if the process dies.
- **Admission.** A request is admitted while the free memory stays above the floor (20 percent of host memory clamped to 4 to 8 GB; 6,553 MB on a 32 GB host), swap stays below 25 percent and swapping out below 64 MB/s, the load stays below 2 per core counting the cores of running grants, memory pressure is not `critical` (`warn` waits), the machine-wide open file table stays below 80 percent and fewer than 32 disk images are attached. The last three and raised pressure are soft: they are waited out and overridden after 120 s only when nothing of ours is running, because a leaked image never goes away by waiting. Memory, swap and critical pressure are never overridden. Estimates: test 1.5 GB, check 4 GB, generate 1 GB, build 2 GB, script 0.75 GB, service 2 GB; `show` and `graph` 5 GB, `dependencies` 6 GB. A request that does not fit gets fewer jobs (`reduced`); one larger than the whole budget runs alone on an idle machine.
- **Grants per tool.** Bazel receives `--local_resources=cpu=N`, `--local_resources=memory=MB` and `--jobs` (and resource sets per action mnemonic, so its scheduler books actions at their estimate), Nx receives `NX_PARALLEL` (it has no memory or core budget, so the mapping is approximate), Cargo receives `--parallelism`. Nested commands inherit the grant of their parent through the environment, so a task that calls `repo` again does not wait for itself.
- **Reading a wait.** A line such as `[capacity] waiting 42s for run: not enough free memory (need 3584 MB; 1200 MB spare above the 6553 MB floor, load 7.1; 2 running, 1 ahead)` names the reason (`memory`, `swap`, `swap-activity`, `pressure-critical`, `pressure-warn`, `files`, `attaches`, `load` or `queue-order`) and the queue position. The line is published every 5 s in English or German; Ctrl+C cancels the wait. The state lives in `~/Library/Caches/semio/host-capacity` (`$XDG_CACHE_HOME` or `~/.cache` on Linux, `%LOCALAPPDATA%` on Windows), shared by every checkout of the user; a grant whose process died or whose boot session ended is reclaimed.
- **Escape hatch.** `SEMIO_HOST_CAPACITY=off` switches the governor off and says so on every command. The governor admits, it does not kill; commands that run outside `repo` are governed only when wrapped in `repo capacity run`.
- **Fairness.** A reservation counts as unrealized, and in the budget at its full estimate, only for the first 5 minutes of its grant (`memory.rampMilliseconds`); afterwards the memory it really holds speaks for itself. A refused request never holds back later requests that fit; it becomes a strict barrier only if finishing our own grants would admit it and it has waited 30 s or been overtaken 6 times, and foreign pressure alone never creates a barrier. A batch request older than 300 s ranks as interactive. The bound on starvation is the bypass time plus one short duration. State: live.
- **Platforms.** macOS reads `vm_stat` and `sysctl`, Linux `/proc/meminfo`, pressure stall information and the cgroup limits, Windows only free physical memory and busy-CPU load (no machine-wide file table, no pressure level). Only macOS was run; the other parsers are tested from fixtures.

### 🔒️ Leases, lock order and queues

Every shared resource is claimed through a lease: a SQLite file `<leaseDir>/<sha256 of the resource name>.sqlite`, plus a `.queue/` directory of tickets for the queued kind. Queued leases (`acquireQueuedResourceLease`) are served in arrival order and a writer is not starved by readers that arrive after it; the raw kind (`acquireResourceLease`) is polled and prefers readers. A waiter at position 2 or later prints a `lease-queue` line every 15 s that names the holder's process id (see Waiting for a store in the reference). The rest of this section is the contract for lock order, deadlocks and the status command (live).

**Lock order.** A resource name falls into one class; a class has a rank, a scope and an owner, all declared in the lock-order table `🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🏃️process/🔒️leases/🧭️order/🔣️policy.json` (a closed schema next to it, English and German messages). A task that holds a lease and asks for a lower rank is out of order. Unranked resources are rank 0 and never constrained.

| Rank | Class | Scope | Resources (examples) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | compiler capacity | machine | `semio.compiler.cpu-picker/v1`, `semio.compiler.cpu/v1/<n>` |
| 2 | tool singletons | checkout | `nx-tooling-*`, `bazel-*` (metrics, snapshot restore, slots), `bun-*`, `rustup:*`, `cache-prune` |
| 2 | Cargo coordination | crate | `cargo:registry:*`, `cargo-preparation:<root>`, `cargo:source-admission` |
| 3 | graph | checkout | `nx:graph-pool:*`, `repository-graph-facts:<workspace>`, `build-graph-*` |
| 4 | namespaces | project | `rust-*`, `javascript-dependency-*`, `typescript-package-binding:*`, `native-snapshot*` |
| 5 | artifacts | path | `artifact:<path>` (shared reads, exclusive publish) |
| 6 | images | whole image | `native-fixed-image:<directory>` (exclusive for sync, install and walks, shared for readers) |
