- **Rules.** *Out of order* is reported (`observe`, the shipped mode) and flips to a hard failure with one line of policy once `repo leases status` shows no violation during the end-to-end battery. *Hold-and-wait* fails a blocking request that has waited longer than 15 s while its task holds another lease exclusively; the message names the held lease, the blocking process, its pid and how long it holds. Shared holds, declared edges and try-locks are exempt. *Self-deadlock* (shared then exclusive on the same lease, or exclusive then anything unbounded) fails at once. *Cycle*: every waiter checks the wait-for graph every 500 ms and exactly one member of a cycle, the latest waiter, fails while the others proceed. `SEMIO_LEASE_ORDER=off|observe|enforce` overrides all four.
- **Waiting no longer blocks admission.** A raw acquire used to keep the shared directory-admission gate while it waited for a busy resource, which blocked every other acquire; it now takes the gate per attempt and waits outside it. `withResourceLeases` acquires in rank-then-name order through the fair queue, so three overlapping readers cannot starve a writer.
- **For authors.** Take the narrowest resource that makes your step correct (per project, crate, generation or path, not per checkout); never hold an exclusive lease while you wait for another; when you introduce or split a key, add a class with its scope and owner to the table. Optional and separate: `SEMIO_LEASE_INHERITED` makes child processes count their parent's holds, which can make previously accepted parent-child nestings fail the self-deadlock and hold-and-wait rules, so it ships after one clean cycle.

```bash
repo leases status
repo leases status --json --violations=50
```

- **`leases status`** shows the holders (mode, class, rank, pid, hold seconds, command), the waiters (queue position, wait seconds, blockers), the wait-for edges, deadlock cycles with their victim, the recent order violations and the machine admission queue of the host capacity governor with each waiter's reason. Options: `--json`, `--all`, `--lease-directory=<path>`, `--violations=<n>`. It reads the per-user registry (`~/Library/Caches/semio/lease-registry`, `$XDG_CACHE_HOME` or `~/.cache`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%`; `SEMIO_LEASE_REGISTRY_DIRECTORY` overrides it; dead pids are pruned, heartbeat 60 s) and the queue tickets, so processes on older code still appear as holders and waiters, without names.
- **Limits.** Task attribution is per invocation, so parallel sub-tasks of one invocation count as one task; deadlock detection sees only processes on the new code; the queued `withResourceLeases` adds claim and queue file churn per acquisition; the compiler capacity picker still holds its lease while it polls cores, so a small request can wait behind an unsatisfiable `exact:N` request.
- **If a command waits today.** Read the `lease-queue` line for the holder's pid and `[capacity]` lines for the governor; both are queues, not hangs. `repo leases status` lists who holds what and who waits for whom.

### 🧵️ Sessions: Bazel slots, Cargo lanes and Nx pools

Each tool had one place where two agents in one folder serialized. This section says what happens today and what the staged sets change; each paragraph carries its state.

**Bazel server slots** (live). A session claims one slot `s0`, `s1`, ... with an exclusive lease; a slot owns one short output base and the generated packages of its selections, and there is exactly one holder per slot, so a server lock wait cannot happen.

- **Slots.** The number of build slots K comes from the host budget: K = clamp(floor((memory minus floor) times 30 percent divided by the resident size of a server), 1 to 6) and at most cores divided by 2; on a 32 GB host with 10 cores that is 3 (16 GB: 1, 128 GB: 6). `SEMIO_BAZEL_SERVERS=<n>` (1 to 99; anything else is refused) overrides it, and the server heap comes from the plan instead of a literal. A session started inside a Bazel action gets 4 overflow slots, so it never waits for the slot its parent holds.
- **Service slots.** A selection in which any task is `continuous` (a dev server, `serve`) holds a service slot `d0`, `d1`, ... for its lifetime (default 4, `SEMIO_BAZEL_SERVICES=<n>`), with its own fair queue; every other selection, including each rebuild cycle of `watch`, is a build, so a queue of waiting dev servers never delays a build and a running service is never rebuilt over. After the launch the server idles out after 120 s, so a pinned dev server costs a slot name and an output base on disk, not a JVM.
- **Affinity and waiting.** A slot remembers its last selection; a session prefers the slot whose last selection it is, then a slot that keeps the package, then a never-used slot, then the least recently used, so an edit-run-edit loop keeps its warm server. When every slot is busy the head of a fair queue polls every 250 ms and prints `[bazel] all 3 Bazel server slots are in use; waiting for one (12 s)` every 5 s (English or German); it honours cancellation. A waiting session holds no store lease: the order is capacity, then slot, then storage.
- **Shared layers.** The disk cache, the repository cache and a new repository contents cache (`<products>/bazel/contents`, one fetch of every external repository for all output bases) are shared by all slots, and action keys do not depend on the output base or the package path (a fresh slot rebuilt the same content with one disk cache hit and no spawn). Restores into the checkout lock the tree they write (`bazel-restore:<path>`, ancestors shared) instead of the whole checkout.
- **Measured** with real Bazel 8.4.2 and three slots: three sessions on three disjoint targets (4 s action each) took 13.3 s and 12.7 s against 35.4 s and 36.5 s summed; a fourth session in a fresh slot had 0 spawns; with three service sessions pinned the builds took 15.2 s; the control (two commands forced into one output base) showed the lock wait. Not verified: Windows and Linux hosts, three servers on the real 134k-target graphs, a real `watch` or `serve` end to end.

**Cargo lanes and the unit cache** (live). A lane is the private target and build directory of one agent, `m/u/l/<id>/{t,b,m,v}`, so no two Cargo processes ever share a build directory.

- **Lanes.** The lane id is `SEMIO_CARGO_LANE`, else derived from the nearest agent-client ancestor process (claude, codex, cursor, windsurf, copilot, antigravity, droid, kiro: one lane per agent session), else `d`. A busy lane overflows into `<id>1` to `<id>31`, which start empty and fill from the unit cache. `--store=shared` means "lane plus shared unit cache"; `--store=isolated` is unchanged. All lanes live inside the issued rust image and are bounded, so the 926 GB of private targets cannot recur. The launcher creates the directories itself and `.cargo/config.toml` names the default lane.
- **Unit cache.** Cargo runs the repository's rustc wrapper, which keys each unit by the toolchain, the normalised command line, the SHA-256 of the root source and of every extern artifact, and then verifies the dependency file rustc wrote (every file it read, by content). A hit restores the outputs by reflink where the file system supports it (APFS, btrfs, XFS; a real copy elsewhere) and replays rustc's JSON messages; a miss compiles once, publishes content-addressed objects and the entry last. Units of the packages selected with `-p` keep `-C incremental` in the lane and are never published.
- **No deadlock, bounded concurrency.** The first process that misses a unit claims its key; the others wait for the finished entry (bounded to 30 minutes, a dead holder is replaced). Host-wide compile slots (`SEMIO_UNIT_CACHE_SLOTS`, default the core count) are served first in first out; the lock order is claim, slot, rustc. The writer custody of the image is shared and escalates to exclusive only to recover missing headroom; registry fetches coalesce per request; the admission result of an unchanged unit is remembered under stamps.
- **Disk.** Idle lanes (24 h) go first; with less than 10 percent of the image free, incremental sets, then lanes, then cache objects are removed, oldest first; the shares are objects 25, lanes 20 and incremental 10 percent, live lanes are never touched. The launcher runs this at most every 10 minutes after a lane is released.
- **Build scripts and component passes** (live). The compiled build script is replaced by a stand-in that keys a run by the SHA-256 of the real executable, the toolchain, its arguments and Cargo's per-package, feature, cfg, target, rustflags and `DEP_*` variables; a hit writes the recorded `OUT_DIR` and replays the recorded `cargo:` lines and warnings without starting the script, a miss runs it once across all lanes and records `OUT_DIR`, both streams and the facts (every `rerun-if-changed` path, every `rerun-if-env-changed` value, the package tree, with `*.rs` ignored when the script declares inputs). A script whose `OUT_DIR` holds links or a binary that embeds the lane path, an output over 256 MiB or a package over 4,096 files runs normally; the stand-in needs a POSIX host, and the launcher exports `SEMIO_UNIT_CACHE_BUILD_SCRIPTS=on`. Component passes (the per-generation builds) use the same wrapper chain: a hit tells the scheduler broker a `cached` unit with the recorded peak memory and CPU, so the unit list, the critical path and the provenance reports stay complete and the scheduling history ignores cached units. 
- **Measured** on a 26-crate wasm workspace with three concurrent sessions: 32 distinct units compiled once instead of about 70, and a fourth session was served 32 of 32 from the cache; the wall time stays dominated by build scripts. Limits: proc-macros that read files without `tracked_path` can serve a stale hit, Windows reports no process ancestry (lane `d` with overflow lanes), reflink falls back to copying on ext4 and NTFS. The deadlock of many Cargo processes on one fine-grain-locked build directory (12 observed) was not reproduced with three; it is removed by construction because no two processes share a directory.

**Nx** (live). A generation receipt binds the three graph files of the digest namespace to the graph digest, the plugin implementation, the Nx tooling and the identity files; when it holds, `NX_FORCE_REUSE_CACHED_GRAPH=true` and Nx re-reads the workspace files but not the graph. Measured on a stock Nx graph of 2,500 projects: 6 to 7 s cold, 0.7 to 1.5 s with the receipt (expected on the live tree: about 24 s to the first task instead of 210 to 350 s). The pool follows the order namespace, then pool: attaching an existing namespace takes no pool, a birth is a short pass that only try-locks the namespace, and eviction runs outside the pool under its own queue. All users hold the fixed cache shared, so a put no longer blocks restores. Two concurrent stock Nx runs over the same 50 hashes, then a put of 150 and a restore of 50, had no failures. Open: the graph file copy of a publication still holds the pool. Daemon policy (live): finite daemons (`run`) are capped at the smaller of 2 and a quarter of the host memory divided by 2 GiB, watch daemons at 6 (`SEMIO_NX_DAEMONS=<finite>[,<watch>]`, 0 turns them off, at most 16); every daemon holds a numbered slot lease `nx:daemon-slot:<root>:<kind>:<k>` for its whole life and refuses to start when its family is full; a run reuses the live daemon of its namespace, is admitted while fewer than the cap are live, and otherwise runs with `NX_DAEMON=false` (graph from the receipt, plain hashing); an explicit `NX_DAEMON` of the caller wins; finite daemons drain after `SEMIO_NX_DAEMON_IDLE_MS` (default 10 minutes) without an open request. Put slots (live): puts no longer take the exclusive compiler-writer custody; they run under shared custody inside one of 4 put slots (`SEMIO_NX_PUT_SLOTS`, 1 to 16) behind a first-in-first-out gate, with the headroom measured as free space minus the maximum artifact size of every other slot in use, so concurrent puts cannot overcommit the image, and they degrade to one at a time when it is tight; the put slots need the shared writer custody of the Cargo set.

**Dependencies** are described under Shared caches and budgets (dependency generations); **TypeScript** under Incremental development.

### 🧪️ Test sandbox

Tests of many agents run at the same time on one machine, so a test must not share anything mutable with another run. Before the sandbox, 21 sites in 12 test files used the generated folder of an old ticket as their scratch root inside the checkout, `repoTestArtifactEnvironment` defaulted to one fixed route in the real cache that every concurrent run of the same route shared, 19 tests used constant names below the temporary directory, and 10 tests probed a port, closed it and bound the number later, which races between agents. A killed run also left an attached disk image whose backing file lay inside the checkout, where nothing ever reclaimed it. State: live.

- **One API.** `createTestSandboxV1`, `withTestSandboxV1`, `testSandboxEnvironmentV1` and `testScratchRootV1` in `🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🏃️process/🧪️testing/🏖️sandbox` (re-exported by the package export `./test-execution`). A sandbox is a private root `<tmpdir>/st/<8 hex>` (mode 0700, length bounded) with `artifacts/<route>`, `tmp`, `images` and `cache`, and an `owner.json` whose heartbeat is refreshed every 30 s. The environment carries `SEMIO_TEST_SANDBOX`, `SEMIO_TEST_ARTIFACT_DIR`, `SEMIO_TEST_IMAGE_ROOT`, `SEMIO_TEST_CACHE_ROOT`, `SEMIO_TEST_CHECKOUT_ROOT`, `SEMIO_TEST_GUARD`, `SEMIO_TEST_GUARD_REPORT` and `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP`. Nested runs adopt the parent's sandbox.
- **It is automatic.** `runBudgetedTestCommand`, the one spawn point of Bun, Node and Go test commands, runs every child inside a per-run sandbox, so `repo run <project>:test` needs nothing from you; with no configured `SEMIO_TEST_ARTIFACT_DIR` the artifact directory is the one of the run's sandbox. Cargo test commands go through the Cargo launcher and get only that store's own `TMPDIR` handling. Go tests were already hermetic (17 owners run under `sandbox.Run`, a temporary git repository as root and working directory).
- **Disposal and reclaim.** The sandbox is disposed by `dispose()`, at process exit and in Bun's `afterAll` (a `bun test` process ends without running exit handlers, so `afterAll` is the one that counts): images below the root are detached, ports are released, the directory is removed. The next sandbox creation (at most every 5 minutes) detaches the images of and removes every sandbox whose owner process is dead or whose heartbeat is older than 1 hour, and reclaims stale port leases. The root and `TMPDIR` lie below the scratch roots of the boot sweep, so an image that even this misses is detached by its 15-minute idle sweep.
- **Ports.** `reservePort()` takes an atomic lease directory per port in the range 49152 to 65000, shared by every sandbox of the machine, bind-checks it and releases it on disposal. Never listen on a literal number and never probe and rebind.
- **The law.** `🧪️testing/🏖️sandbox/🧪️tests/⚖️law` scans the test sources of every manifest-owned directory (23 rules in TypeScript, Go, Rust, Python and shell, with English and German messages): writes into the real checkout, fixed temporary names, `process.cwd()` writers, disk-image and kill commands on non-sandbox paths, fixed ports, probe-then-rebind. Its error findings must equal the ledger `🧪️testing/🏖️sandbox/🧪️tests/🧫️fixtures/📒️ledger.json` exactly: a new violator fails, and a fixed one fails until its row is removed, so the ledger only shrinks. 31 rows remain, each with its reason (scratch that must lie below the workspace, the vitest lane, Rust fixed-name tests, the mcp adapters that build into the shared binary cache). The TypeScript scanner and a separate Python reference produce the same 1,042 findings over the tree, and ripgrep marks the same lines for every line rule.
- **Runtime guard.** `SEMIO_TEST_GUARD=off|report|enforce` (default `off`) preloads a shim for `node:fs`, `node:fs/promises` and `node:child_process` imports of repository TypeScript. It refuses or records writes inside the real checkout (`.cache`, `.🧬semio`, `.git`, `node_modules` are protected; `.cache/b/o/m/tests` is allowed), `hdiutil` and `diskutil` verbs on non-sandbox paths, `pkill` and `killall`, and fixed ports that the sandbox did not lease. Rows are JSONL (schema `GuardReportRow`) and summarised when the sandbox is disposed. Run a sample with `SEMIO_TEST_GUARD=report repo run <project>:test` and read the report before turning enforcement on; the default stays `off` until the loader rewrite has run across the 2,175 suites.
- **Verified** with 16 sandbox tests (349 expectations: 12 ports across two sandboxes and three child processes all distinct, a SIGKILLed child with an attached image reclaimed by the next sweep, the guard in both modes) and the law; the five envelope-gated converted suites and the whole battery under `report` were not run, nor Windows and Linux.

### 🪞️ Parity verification

Nx and Bazel must produce the same results from the same neutral graph. `repo verify build-parity` proves it per sample instead of by inspection (live). `verify` is a root command, not a build operation: it first runs `repo dependencies ensure`, and where `repo` is not on the `PATH` it is `bun ./📜️script.ts verify build-parity` at the root.

```bash
repo verify build-parity --plans-only
repo verify build-parity --only=area:framework
repo verify build-parity --samples=<file> --out=<directory> --keep --json
repo verify build-parity --recover=<directory>
repo inputs @teaching/architecture-course:test
```

- **What one sample does.** The dry-run task graph of both tools and the closure (Bazel `cquery` against the planner); then `repo --tool=nx|bazel run <target>` fresh (`--skip-cache`) and restored from the cache; the declared outputs of each tool captured into separate roots and compared byte for byte (kind, mode, link target, SHA-256); exit codes, failed tasks and test status; the Nx `inputs` against the inputs and environment of Bazel's spawns (read from its execution log); and the cache restore of each tool cold against restored.
- **Only a rule explains a difference.** `🏗️build/🪞️parity/🔣️rules.json` holds 8 normalisations, each with a justification in English and German and its evidence, and the report counts how often each applied. Everything else is a divergence (35 codes, each with a help text) and the command exits non-zero. 27 samples in 6 areas (`🏗️build/🪞️parity/🔣️samples.json`: `framework`, `s-plugins`, `hub`, `teaching`, `mit-bestand`, `semio-tech`) are authored; `--only=<id>` or `--only=area:<area>` (comma separated) selects, `--samples=<file>` replaces them.
- **Safe for your tree.** Declared output roots are set aside before each run and restored in a `finally` (also on Ctrl+C); tracked roots are copied, untracked ones moved; `--recover=<directory>` restores from the journal after a crash. Progress is published per sample, tool and phase and every step is a cancellation checkpoint.
- **Reports.** `report.json`, `report.en.md` and `report.de.md` land in the evidence directory (`--out`, kept with `--keep`); `--lang=en|de` selects the help language. The Bazel side records its evidence (execution log, build events, closure) only when `SEMIO_BUILD_EVIDENCE_DIR` is set, which the verifier sets for its own Bazel runs.
- **`repo inputs <project>:<target>[:<configuration>]`** prints what Nx hashes for one task (files, environment, runtime values, dependency outputs, external packages) as JSON. Bazel does not implement it: its inputs are the spawn inputs in the parity evidence.
- **Not yet verified.** The real-tool answers: it was tested with fixtures, a fake `repo`, and Bazel evidence tests; the first live runs (the course build, the semio-tech play build, the mit-bestand demonstrator release build, a compact report print) are open. A heavy sample such as the demonstrator release build can exceed the 3,600 s sample limit. Run one process at a time; the host capacity governor applies to the child runs.

### 🥾️ Recovery after a reboot or a crash

A reboot, a kernel panic or a killed run leaves state behind: publication intents of a killed publisher, queue tickets of dead processes, disk images of finished tests, grants of the host capacity governor and device numbers that no longer mean what they meant. The boot sweep (live) recovers all of it at the next `repo` command, with progress and cancellation, and never blocks the command if a recovery step fails. You do not run a recovery command.

1. **Run the command you wanted.** The first partition open of the process runs the sweep. Its steps publish `boot-recovery:<stage>:<done>/<total>:<target>` progress lines; a failed step is reported as `boot-recovery:failed:<reason>` and the command continues; Ctrl+C cancels the sweep and the command.
2. **What the sweep does.**

| Leftover | Recovery |
|---|---|
| `.publication/intent.json` or `receipt.json` of a killed publisher (every command refused with "Pending Native image publication must settle") | a pure plan (rolled back, completed before the crash, torn, empty) executed under the exclusive image lease the opener already takes; ambiguous or unknown residue is refused with its reason and never deleted |
| queue tickets and claim databases of dead processes in `.cache/b/c` | tickets of an earlier boot are retired one by one, only after the repository's own exclusive claim-database proof; tickets of a dead process in the same boot are reaped by the next waiter behind them; once per boot, recorded in a ledger |
| disk images of finished or killed tests (the scratch-image class) and images whose backing file vanished (macOS) | an image under a scratch root (`/private/tmp`, `/private/var/folders`, `/var/tmp`, `/tmp`, `$TMPDIR`, `$SEMIO_TEST_ARTIFACT_DIR`), outside this workspace, idle for 15 minutes and with its own lease provably free is detached without force; a busy volume is skipped; workspace and foreign images are never touched; at most one sweep per 5 minutes |
| device numbers stored in receipts (they change after a reboot or a parent image re-attach) | no longer persisted truth: inode, mode, size and platform stay exact and devices are compared only through their pairing; image receipts are rebound to the new boot session without rewriting them |
| grants of the capacity governor of an earlier boot or of a dead process | reclaimed when the owner dies or the boot session changes |
| sandboxes and port leases of dead test runs | reclaimed by the next sandbox creation (dead owner, or heartbeat older than 1 hour) |

3. **What the sweep leaves.** Funded compiler-state children (an Nx namespace) with a reserved row and no commit stay until their owner resumes them; stale Bazel and Nx server files (`server.pid.txt`, cookies) are left to the tools' own stale-server handling; a reboot inside a live Bazel or Nx server session is not repaired by the sweep.
4. **Check afterwards.** `repo capacity status` lists no grant of the previous boot; `repo dependencies ensure` installs only what is missing. For the one refusal that still names a state ("Exact canonical persistent Native publication required …") and for the Nx cache, follow Recovery in the reference: use the dashboard control that owns the operation.
5. **Never** detach an image by hand, force an unmount, edit or move a receipt, or delete a directory below `.cache/b`. Cache partitions, Bazel output bases and the Cargo store are derived and safe to remove only when nothing runs and only through their owning controls (`repo cache clear`, the dashboard).

Verified in copies of the tree with real `hdiutil` (an abandoned and a fresh scratch image, a SIGKILL before each state-changing call and every torn write of the publication ceremony, the directory swap, a record transaction and the ledger append: recovery reaches the old or the new receipt and the second run is clean); the sweep measured 17 ms for the roster and 10 ms for a no-op. Not verified: a live drill after a real reboot, Linux and Windows (the boot epoch parsers are tested for all three platforms from fixtures).

### 🖥️ Cross-platform notes

| Host | Notes |
|---|---|
| macOS | Cache partitions are case-sensitive APFS images, no privilege. Keep the checkout path ASCII (Bazel crashes at startup on a non-ASCII install base and its sandbox mis-encodes a non-ASCII output base; the layout is refused up front). The adapter sets a UTF-8 locale. |
| Linux | Directory-backed partitions, no root and no loop device; needs a case-sensitive file system with atomic directory exchange (ext4, xfs, btrfs). A non-ASCII `install` path is refused; a non-ASCII output base works. In an unprivileged container Bazel falls back to `processwrapper-sandbox` (same results, weaker isolation); `linux-sandbox` needs a privileged container. The adapter never names a host-specific sandbox strategy: hermetic actions list `sandboxed`, which Bazel resolves to the best sandbox the host registers (`darwin-sandbox`, `linux-sandbox`, `processwrapper-sandbox`); naming `linux-sandbox` itself is refused by name in an unprivileged container. |
| Devcontainer | As Linux. `.cache` is a named volume private to the checkout (a bind mount from a case-insensitive host is refused by `repo preflight`); `postCreateCommand` runs `repo run workspace:setup`. |
| Windows | Directory partitions swapped by a journaled rename on NTFS with per-directory case sensitivity. Needs `LongPathsEnabled` and a short ASCII checkout root (the Bazel path budget is `len(output base) + len(\execroot\_main\s\) + 256` against 259, or 32766 with long paths). The source link is a junction (no developer mode, no elevation), runfiles are off (`--noenable_runfiles`, startup `--nowindows_enable_symlinks`), so tests run as cached actions instead of `bazel test`, and hermetic actions are remote-only. Set variables with `$env:NAME = "value"` in PowerShell and call `bun repo`. Not run on Windows. |

No cache path may exceed 256 characters on any host. Bazel and Bun are pinned per OS and CPU (darwin, linux, windows on arm64 and x64) with checksums, so `repo tools setup` needs no manual download.

### 🩹️ Troubleshooting

- **A command refuses after a reboot or image reattachment** ("Exact canonical persistent Native publication required …"). Publication receipts remain immutable. Device numbers are scoped to the current kernel namespace and can change after a reboot or a parent image reattachment within the same boot. A current publication is observed through the freshly anchored backing device while its published inode, mode, required ancestor chain, physical limits and kernel association remain exact; this does not rewrite a receipt or transfer deletion authority. An unsupported receipt is refused. The fixed **Reset Inactive Nx Cache** control covers only the canonical inactive macOS Nx capsule; other changed, mounted, busy or unknown states remain diagnostic refusals (Recovery).
- **A command waits.** Stores are claimed through a first-in-first-out lease queue. A waiter at position 2 or later prints a `lease-queue` line every 15 seconds that names the holder's process id; a line `remains active on …` is progress of a running walk, not a wait. Do not kill a holder that is making progress; read the head ticket first. A line that starts with `[capacity]` comes from the host capacity governor, not from a lease: it names the reason and your queue position (Host capacity); `repo leases status` lists who holds what and who waits for whom. A dependency sync or a cold graph build holds its image or its facts lease for a long time (Leases, lock order and queues).
- **A receipt fails.** Store receipt: one task re-runs uncached (`[store-receipt] re-executing`). Dependency generation receipt: an edit of any file in its closure costs the next command one complete walk (progress `dependencies:generation-verify:<n>`). Image receipt: see the first item.
- **No cache hit.** Check in this order: `--skip-cache` or the dashboard axis `cache` set to a skip value; the target's decision in the manifest (`unclassified` never replays); an undeclared or too wide input; the mode (`prototype` vs `release`); another process editing declared inputs during the build; for a remote hit a different user or checkout path.
- **Remote cache.** `repo cache remote status` shows `configured: false` when no endpoint is set, `reachable: false` when the endpoint does not answer and `authorized: false` for a wrong token (Nx aborts a run on 401, ignores 403 on upload silently).
- **Workers.** Rerun with `--workers off` to separate a worker problem from a task problem. A build that fails with "Captured implementation Source changed" saw a build-tool file change under it; rerun it.
- **Installed packages.** `Cannot find module` for a workspace package means the dependencies are older than the sources: `repo dependencies lock --all`, then `repo dependencies sync --all`. On a fresh clone the Nx setup may need the installed workspace links: if it stops with `Cannot find module`, run the sync first.
- **Nx reports `.nx/installation` missing.** Run `repo tools setup nx`.

### ✅️ Verified and not yet verified

Verified on 2026-10-20 (macOS arm64, Bazel 8.4.2, Nx 23.2.0; Linux arm64 container for the platform rows): real-Bazel cold, warm, after-`clean` and after-server-loss runs with the production task owner, early cutoff, tests as cached `bazel test`, `affected` against Bazel `rdeps` and Nx's locators, real Bazel and a pristine Nx against `repo cache serve` (remote hits after clearing the local cache, wrong and read-only tokens), persistent workers against real Bazel, the hermetic law, the Linux container setup, and the fixture suites of every component. Not yet verified: a production `repo --tool=bazel run` after the integration, Nx with the repository's patched install against a remote cache, a real remote executor and remote execution of any repository target, Windows, the devcontainer lifecycle, and every end-to-end chain under both tools (see Verification status).

Verified in copies of the tree on 2026-10-20, not yet through `repo` on the live checkout: the lock order with real child processes (deadlock victims, starvation, a wait-for graph against a SQLite oracle), Bazel server slots with real Bazel 8.4.2, Cargo lanes and the unit cache with real Cargo and sccache as oracle, graph generations against an independent Python model, the Nx graph receipt and concurrent writers against stock Nx 23.2.0, concurrent dependency generations with real Bun, the cache ledger against a SQLite oracle, TypeScript check units against `tsc -p`, the host capacity decision against an independent SQLite implementation, the incremental and concurrency judges (29 and 26 tests, with real Bazel), `repo verify build-parity` with fixtures and a fake `repo`, the boot sweep with real `hdiutil` and SIGKILL injection, and the test sandbox (16 tests; its law finds the same 1,042 findings in TypeScript and in a separate Python reference). Not yet verified: a production `repo --tool=bazel run`, a cold `repo show projects` on the new graph code, `repo incremental prepare` and its first judged scenarios, the first `repo concurrency run`, `repo verify build-parity` against the real tools, every staged set on the live tree, and Linux, Windows and the devcontainer for all of them.

## 🧭️ Neutral model

| Layer | What it is | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Manifest | One `📋️project.json` per project: targets with executor, command, inputs, outputs, `cache`, `continuous`, `dependsOn`. | schema `📚️library/🏗️build/🧬️schema/📋️project/🔣️.json` |
| Policy | `defaultTool`, the named input groups (`default`, `production`, `sharedGlobals`, `cargo`, `go`, `uv`, `dotnet`, `schemaSources`, `nativeSources`, `nativeTestSources`) and the files that may change the graph. | `📚️library/🏗️build/🔣️policy.json` |
| Graph | `BuildGraphV1`: projects, targets and dependency edges inferred in TypeScript from the manifests, Cargo, Go, `package.json` and `bun.lock`; independent of Nx. Snapshots live in the graph partition `g`. `show` and `graph` print it. | `📚️library/🏗️build/🕸️graph` |
| Operations | The catalogue of operations, which tool implements each one how, and the JSON shapes their results must have. | `📚️library/🏗️build/⚙️operations/🔣️catalog.json`, schema `⚙️operations/🧬️schema/🔣️.json` |
| Implementations | Nx adapter and Bazel adapter; each one only translates the neutral request into its tool. | Sections Nx implementation and Bazel implementation below. |

**Tool selection.** `--tool=nx|bazel` on the command line wins, then the environment variable `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL`, then `defaultTool` of the policy (`nx`). The selected tool is exported as `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL` to every nested task, so a task that calls `repo` again keeps the tool; the dashboard axis `build-tool` sets the same variable (see Command registry). Anything else is refused ("Unknown build tool"). Choose the compiler configuration with `--configuration=prototype|release` (or `-c`, a `:<configuration>` suffix on the task, or `SEMIO_BUILD_MODE`); disagreeing values are refused ("Conflicting build modes", stated in English and German, naming the fixed-mode targets such as `dev` that pin a mode). The mode is one invocation axis, not a per-target setting: the Bazel implementation accepts `prototype` and `release` for every target whether or not the target declares them as configurations, every planned task of every target carries the mode in its id, label and workspace, the dependencies of the selected task inherit the mode, the original caller's mode is the authority every task environment exports (`SEMIO_BUILD_MODE`) and is part of every action key, and the plan refuses a task whose mode differs from the caller's and a dependency whose target is fixed to another mode. Only `prototype` and `release` can be selected at the command line; other configuration names exist only in the Bazel adapter. The course verbs that need release (`publish`, `test-e2e` rehearsal, `docker-image-build`, `docker-image-publish`, `deploy-check` of `@teaching/architecture-course`) read the original caller's mode and refuse a prototype caller in English and German; select release with `bun repo [--tool=bazel] run @teaching/architecture-course:publish:release`.

For an exact `run <canonical-project-name>:<target>` selection, `repo` reads `📋️build-owners.json` and that owner’s JSON manifest before issuing the original compiler mode. A target may declare `metadata.semio.buildMode.fixed` as `prototype` or `release`; this is a constraint and conflicting selections are refused. `defaultConfiguration` is a fallback used when no mode was explicitly selected or inherited. Named fixed workflows keep their mode. Unknown owners, inferred targets and Cargo manifests add no authored mode. `run-many` and `affected` retain backend-owned project selectors, exclusions and changed-file filtering; they use the explicitly selected or inherited mode, named workflow mode, or the generic prototype fallback without reading owner declarations.

**Operations.** The state of each operation per tool: `native` = the tool's own command, `adapter` = our code drives the tool, `neutral` = tool independent, `missing` = not implemented and the call refuses.

| Operation | Nx | Bazel | `--dry-run` | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `run <project:target[:configuration]>` | native | adapter | yes | A dry run executes nothing. |
| `run-many --targets=… [--projects=…]` | native | adapter | yes | |
| `affected --targets=…` | native | adapter | yes | Changed files are the tracked changes against `HEAD` plus untracked files; `--base`/`--head`, `--files`, `--uncommitted`, `--untracked` narrow them and `--all` selects every project. Affected projects (the same rule for both tools, asserted against Nx's own locators): the deepest project root that owns a changed file, projects whose target inputs name a changed `{workspaceRoot}/…` file, every project when `nx.json` changes, then all dependents. `--projects` is refused for every tool; use `run-many` to narrow. |
| `exec --projects=… -- <command>` | native | adapter | yes | Once per selected project, working directory is the project root, and the tool-neutral pair `SEMIO_PROJECT_NAME` (project name) and `SEMIO_PROJECT_ROOT` (project root relative to the workspace) is exported by both tools; the Nx implementation maps the names its own `exec` exports (see Nx implementation). Under Bazel each project gets an uncached `repo-exec` task. `--dry-run` prints the `repo-exec` plan and runs nothing. |
| `watch --projects=… -- <command>` | neutral | neutral | no | Reruns the command when selected source files change. |
| `graph`, `show projects\|project <name>\|targets` | neutral | neutral | no | Reads the portable graph; never starts Nx or Bazel, by design. |
| `reset` | adapter | adapter | no | Nx: invalidates the graph and runs `nx reset`. Bazel: invalidates the graph and empties `bazel/actions` (the disk cache), `bazel/outputs` and `bazel/workspaces` of the products partition. |
| `tools setup [nx\|bazel\|all]` | adapter | adapter | no | Without an argument the selected tool is set up (`--tool`, default `nx`). See Setup per platform below. `tools setup bazel` needs neither the JavaScript install nor Nx and opens only the tools and dependencies partitions; the products partition is opened by the first run. |
| `preflight` | neutral | neutral | no | Checks the host before any store is provisioned, from `📚️library/🏗️build/🩺️preflight/🔣️policy.json`: CPU architecture, 8 GiB free, required tools (`cc` and `pkg-config` on Linux, `cc` and `hdiutil` on macOS) and, on Linux, atomic directory exchange (`renameat2` `RENAME_EXCHANGE`) on a case-sensitive workspace file system. |
| `dependencies sync\|lock --all\|--scope <owner>` | neutral | neutral | no | Bun installs the JavaScript dependencies for both tools, by design. |
| `cargo …` | neutral | neutral | no | The repository's compile ingress; Bazel's Cargo actions call it, so it is the leaf of both tools, by design. Run Rust targets with `run <project>:build\|check\|test` to use Bazel's cache. |
| `cache status\|clear\|remote status\|serve` | adapter | adapter | no | Nx: the artifact directory of the rust partition. Bazel: the disk cache of the products partition. See the two implementation sections. `remote status` and `serve` belong to the remote cache (see Remote cache). |

**Flags.** Flags before `--` are portable and validated before any tool starts; an unknown flag, a stray positional argument or an invalid selector is refused. Everything after `--` is passed literally to the task. The flag list is part of the catalogue and is printed by `repo --help`; the task commands (`run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `exec`, `watch`) accept all flags of the table, but a flag only has an effect where the last column names its command.

| Flag | Meaning | Commands |
|---|---|---|
| `--tool=nx\|bazel` | Select the implementation (otherwise `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL`, then the policy default). | all |
| `--workers=on\|off` | Run actions in persistent workers (`on`) or one process each (`off`); otherwise `SEMIO_BUILD_WORKERS`, then the worker policy. | all |
| `--help`, `-h`; `--lang=en\|de` | Print the usage of the command in English or German and stop. | all |
| `--projects`, `-p`, `--exclude` | Comma lists of project names with `*` wildcards, `tag:<tag>`, `name:<name>`, `directory:<root>`, `!<pattern>` negation. | `run-many`, `exec`, `watch`; `--exclude` also `affected` |
| `--targets`, `--target`, `-t` | Target names or `*` patterns. | `run-many`, `affected` |
| `--configuration`, `-c` | Compiler configuration (`cargo` takes the full name only). | `run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `cargo` |
| `--parallel=<n>`, `--bail`, `--verbose`, `--output=stream\|stream-without-prefixes\|static` (alias `--output-style`) | Concurrency, stop at first failure, detail, output style. | `run`, `run-many`, `affected` |
| `--skip-cache`, `--skip-remote-cache`, `--exclude-dependencies` (alias `--exclude-task-dependencies`) | Ignore the task cache, ignore the remote cache, run only the named tasks. | `run`, `run-many`, `affected` |
| `--dry-run`, `--graph[=<file>]` | Print the task graph as a `DryRunPlan` on stdout and execute nothing. `--graph` is the same flag on both tools; with a file the plan is written there (Nx: the file as given, Bazel: a report whose path is printed). | `run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `exec` |
| `--all`, `--base`, `--head`, `--files`, `--uncommitted`, `--untracked` | Change selection (`--all`: every project). | `affected`; `--all` also `watch` and `dependencies` |
| `--include-global-files` | `watch` also reacts to files outside any project. | `watch` |
| `--file=<path>`, `--json` | Write the result as a report file; print lists as JSON. | `graph`, `show` |
| `--scope <owner>` | Restrict the operation to one dependency owner (instead of `--all`). | `dependencies` |
| `--store=shared\|isolated`, `--parallelism=<cores>` | Cargo target store and core count. | `cargo` |
| `--port`, `--host`, `--directory`, `--max-bytes` | Port, address, store directory and size bound of the remote cache server (`--name value` or `--name=value`). | `cache serve` |

**Result shapes.** Results that scripts consume are specified by the schema `⚙️operations/🧬️schema/🔣️.json`:

- `DryRunPlan`: `{"tasks":{"roots":[<id>],"tasks":{<id>:{"id":…,"target":{"project":…,"target":…,"configuration"?:…}}},"dependencies":{<id>:[<id>]},"continuousDependencies"?:{<id>:[<id>]}}}`. A task id is `<project>:<target>[:<configuration>]`; `exec` uses the target `repo-exec`. The Bazel plan adds `selected` (the requested task ids) and lists dependencies on continuous tasks apart in `continuousDependencies`, because those only have to be running.
- `CacheStatus`: `{"version":1,"tool","available","directory","entries","bytes","activity"?:{"builds","actions","executed","cacheHits"}}` (`activity` is filled by Bazel). `CacheClear`: `{"version":1,"tool","cleared","removedEntries","removedBytes"}`.

## 📋️ Manifests

A task in `targets` of a `📋️project.json` uses these fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `executor` | `repo:script` (default, runs `command` through the repo process authority) or `repo:noop` (ordering only, runs nothing). |
| `command`, `options.command`, `options.commands`, `options.args` | What runs; `args` are literal arguments that are never shell-interpolated. `configurations.<name>` may override `command`, `commands`, `cwd`, `env`, `args`. `defaultConfiguration` names the configuration used when none is requested. |
| `dependsOn` | Tasks that must finish first: `"target"` (same project), `"^target"` (dependency projects), `"project:target"`, or `{target, projects, dependencies, params}`. |
| `inputs` | What the result depends on, see below. |
| `outputs` | Paths the task writes, relative tokens `{projectRoot}`, `{workspaceRoot}` and option tokens such as `{options.env.SEMIO_BUILD_MODE}`. `[]` means "no files to restore". |
| `cache` | Boolean, see the classes below. |
| `continuous` | The task is a long-running service. |
| `metadata.semio.dashboard.ready` | How readiness of a continuous task is detected: the output shows `http://(127.0.0.1\|localhost\|0.0.0.0):<port>` and that address answers 2xx or 3xx; `port` or `portEnv` declares the port, `path` appends a path, `printed` takes the whole printed address. |

**Inputs.** A list of file globs (`{projectRoot}/**/*`, `{workspaceRoot}/…`, `!` excludes), named groups from the policy (`default`, `production`, `cargo`, …), `^<group>` (the same group of the dependency projects), and objects: `{"env": "NAME"}`, `{"runtime": "<command>"}` (the command's output), `{"externalDependencies": [...]}` and `{"dependentTasksOutputFiles": "<glob>", "transitive": true|false}` (the outputs of the tasks in `dependsOn`). Declare everything the task reads that is not reachable from its own project: data outside the project, spawned test files, tool and toolchain files, environment. A project can add its own `namedInputs` (for example `reads`, `testEnv`, `cargoToolchain`). `default` of a project replaces the workspace `default`, so re-add `sharedGlobals` (platform, Bun version, `SEMIO_BUILD_MODE`) there.

**Command source closure.** A `repo:script` target needs no hand-written list of the modules its script imports. The graph inference derives the closure of the command script (`📚️library/🏗️build/🕸️graph/📥️inference/🧭️script-inputs`): relative imports, imports through workspace package specifiers such as `@semio-tech/process/leases` (`workspacePackageModuleV1`; installed registry packages stay covered by their locked external dependency), literal `new URL("…", import.meta.url)` targets that exist (modules are followed, other files are hashed as resources), and literal module inputs a target declares (for example `{projectRoot}/…/x.test.ts`). The closure joins the target as named inputs `commandSources…`; in the hash representation of the graph it is the marker `{"env": "SEMIO_SOURCE_CLOSURE_<sha256 of the manifest>"}` and the manifest sits in the graph store under `build-input-closures`, which Nx reads when it hashes the task. Cargo lock projections are markers of the same kind (`SEMIO_CARGO_LOCK_<key>`). Bazel expands the markers itself (see Bazel implementation). A target opts into more derived closure with `metadata.semio.closure`: `modules` lists `{projectRoot}`/`{workspaceRoot}` globs of module files (a test directory the router never imports) whose matches join as entries together with their import closure, and `typescript` lists tsconfig paths whose compiler program (include set, path mappings, `.d.ts` siblings, type-only imports, `extends` chain) joins the closure and names the installed type packages it resolved. References such as `new URL("x.json", origin(import.meta.url))` and `join`/`resolve(<module directory>, "x.json")` with literal segments join as files. Deliberately not covered: in-source test imports guarded by `import.meta.vitest`, files read through computed paths, and targets that are never cached (continuous, live). The course keeps this honest with `🏗️build/🕸️graph/📥️inference/🧪️tests/🎓️course-inputs` (esbuild, the TypeScript program and an independent Python implementation against the manifest). Measured on a sample of 49 project scripts the closure grew from a median of 489 to 1,200 files (about 28 percent more CPU for the derivation, offset by an admission memo that cut the old cost by 35 percent). Not yet verified: the assembled inputs in a real graph load (`repo show projects` was stopped on 2026-10-20 after 4 hours; the later analysis found it garbage-collection bound under host memory starvation, not quadratic in the closure code: a project costs 0.3 to 0.45 CPU-s, the closure derivation takes about 29 percent of that, and a clean run is estimated at 25 to 40 minutes), an Nx hash of a real task, and a Bazel run with expanded closures against the production entry.

**Cache classes.** `cache` is the switch both tools read. Next to it the inference attaches a machine-readable decision `metadata.semio.cache = {"version":1,"class":…,"reason":…}` to every target it forces, synthesizes or refuses to replay (closed schema `📚️library/🏗️build/🏃️execution/🧬️schema/💾️cache.json`). The class names the physical fact that allows or forbids a replay:

| Class | Reasons | Meaning | Cached |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deterministic` | `pure-function-of-inputs`, `check-without-outputs` | A pure function of its declared inputs; every declared output is a plain restorable tree, or there is none. | yes, the only replayable class |
| `driver-owned` | `freshness-owned-by-driver` | The command compiles through the build driver, whose Cargo freshness and stage cache are the authority (playground `prepare-*` and `build-*-react*`, `component`). | no |
| `managed-output` | `outputs-are-store-links`, `outputs-in-managed-store` | Outputs live in the leased products store behind a binding or manifest protocol (TypeScript artifact `build`, wasm slots) and the manifest does not author `cache: true`; restoring a copy would be refused or bypass the store. With `cache: true` the target is `deterministic` instead, because its receipt is the restorable output (see Store receipts). | no |
| `continuous` | `serves-until-stopped` | Runs until stopped. | no |
| `side-effecting` | `publishes-live-state`, `publishes-remote`, `mutates-workspace`, `mutates-host-store` | Mutates state outside its declared outputs. | no |
| `live` | `drives-running-process`, `exercises-host-resources` | Observes or drives a running process (names with `e2e` or `live`), or exercises the real locks, storage, process tree or clock of this machine. | no |
| `aggregate` | `orders-prerequisites`, `reenters-build-tool` | Orders prerequisites and does no work of its own. | no |
| `diagnostic` | `admission-refusal`, `measures-environment`, `reads-caller-input` | Reports a refusal or a measurement and is always re-evaluated. | no |
| `unclassified` | `no-cache-classification` | No row, declaration or verifying command classifies the target, so it never replays until the policy names its kind (fail-closed). | no |

The Bazel adapter reads the decision (`bazelTargetCacheClassV1` validates it against the closed schema and refuses an invalid one with "Closed repository cache decision required") and derives its cache decision from the class, see the Bazel cache decision below. The Nx projection does not read it; Nx decides from `cache`, `continuous` and `outputs`. A manifest may declare it itself (a target with `cache: false` records its reason this way, see the Build tools guide); the synthesized playground targets, the TypeScript artifact `test`, `check` and (with `cache: true`) `build` targets, and the wasm slot producers with `cache: true` carry `deterministic`, targets cached by the name rules below carry the class of their row. The policy that sets `cache` and the decision follows this order, the first matching row wins (`📚️library/🏗️build/🕸️graph/📥️inference/🎯️target-policy`, names from `📚️library/⚡️caching/🔣️policy.json`):

| Order | Rule | Cached | Decision attached |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `continuous: true`, or a name `dev`, `serve`, `watch`, `start`, `test-watch`, `daemon` (also `<name>-…`) | no | `continuous` |
| 2 | `cache: false` in the manifest | no | none |
| 3 | A name of the `uncached` list (`audit`, `bench`, `clean`, `deploy`, `deps`, `install`, `publish`, `seed`, `setup`, `test-e2e`, `update`, …) or an exact name (`format`, `commit`, `os`, `parity`, …); `cache: true` is overridden | no | `live` (`e2e`, `live`, `os`, `semio`), `diagnostic` (`audit`, `bench`, `doctor`, `parity`, …), `side-effecting` (`publish`, `deploy` as `publishes-remote`, otherwise `mutates-workspace`) |
| 4 | `hub-live-catalog-check`, `hub-live-catalog-native-check`, `hub-live-catalog-oracle` | yes | none (exceptions to row 5) |
| 5 | A dash-separated word `clean`, `gc`, `prune`, `setup`, `fuzz`, `e2e` or `live` in the name | no | as row 3 |
| 6 | The decision the target declares in `metadata.semio.cache`, else the first matching row of `targetClasses` in `📚️library/⚡️caching/🔣️policy.json` (ordered rows of exact names, heads, tails and tokens, each with a decision; an exact name beats the rest): `deterministic/check-without-outputs` for the heads `test`, `check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `format-check`, `verify`, `schema`, `stdio`, a trailing `-check`, `-test`, `-oracle`, `-lint`, `-source` or `-native`, the token `contract` and a list of exact names; `deterministic/pure-function-of-inputs` for the heads `build` and `generate` and a list of exact producers; `side-effecting/mutates-workspace` for the head `generator` | yes when the class is `deterministic`, otherwise no | the class of the row; for `deterministic` the default inputs `default` and `^default`, outputs `[]` unless the manifest says otherwise |
| 7 | A `verify` command in the target | yes | `deterministic/check-without-outputs` |
| 8 | Anything else | **no** | `unclassified/no-cache-classification` |

Row 8 is fail-closed under both tools: a target that nothing classifies runs every time, whatever its `cache` field says (`unclassified`); the cache-correctness law (`📚️library/⚖️laws/🔒️cache-correctness`) reports it together with every `cache: true` that the policy overrode and every `cache: false` without a recorded reason. A `deterministic/pure-function-of-inputs` target that declares no output is demoted to `side-effecting/mutates-workspace` before either tool sees it (`cachedOutputsAdmittedV1`), because a hit would replay an empty success. Bazel additionally requires an `outputs` array and a class that allows replay, see its cache decision. Write `cache: false` plus a decision for these reasons (the convention across the manifests, mirrored by the classes above): tests that exercise real storage, lease, process or toolchain state; live host, browser or server runs; writers that rewrite the tree or a store outside their declared outputs; launchers of long-lived tools; network downloads; steps whose input set is unbounded (digests of the whole workspace).

**Store receipts.** Products that are published into a leased, content-addressed store instead of the project directory (Cargo artifacts in the compiler store, Vite sites, wasm packages, TypeScript artifacts) cannot be restored by copying files into that store from outside. They declare a receipt as their only output, `{projectRoot}/.🧾️store/<target>.json` (schema `📚️library/🏗️build/🧾️store-receipt/🧬️schema/🔣️.json`, git-ignored, deliberately not below `dist`, which is a link into the store for TypeScript artifacts). The receipt names each published store path (`entries`: kind, bytes, files, SHA-256; a directory digest hashes its sorted file list) and each link that binds a project path to a store path (`links`). The central publishers write it after publication, so no build script changes: the artifact destination that the Vite and wasm publishers write through (`⚡️caching/📦️artifacts/🟦️.ts`), the compiler store publisher of Cargo `build` (`⚡️caching/📦️artifacts/🏗️native-build/🟦️.ts`, skipped when an observation output is requested) and the TypeScript artifact binder (`⚡️caching/📦️artifacts/🟦️typescript/🔗️binding/🟦️.ts`). Both adapters hand the receipt location to the one task that declares it (`SEMIO_TASK_RECEIPT`, issued by `issueStoreReceiptV1` in `📚️library/🏗️build/🧾️store-receipt/🟨️.mjs`; Nx: `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🔌️nx-plugin/📤️arguments/🟨️.mjs`, Bazel: `executeTask` in `📚️library/🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/📜️script.ts`); every other task has it unset, so a nested task never records into its parent's receipt, and a publisher does nothing without it (`recordStoreContentV1`).

After every `run` or `run-many`, both tools verify the receipt of each selected task and of its prerequisites. A receipt that is absent, damaged or points at a missing or changed store entry counts as a miss: that one task reruns alone and uncached (Nx: `run <task> --skip-nx-cache --excludeTaskDependencies`, the one extra child selection the caller admits, which also replaces the cache entry; Bazel: in-process with `skipCache` and without dependencies, which leaves the stale disk-cache entry in place, so a later hit repairs again) and is verified again, and the invocation fails closed otherwise. A failed first run is retried once after the repair. A task that declares a receipt but whose handler wrote none is an error, not silently accepted. TypeScript artifact `build` and wasm slot producers carry the receipt as their output: they replay when the manifest authors `cache: true` (decision `deterministic`) and stay `managed-output` otherwise. A target declares a receipt only when its `outputs` list the receipt path (`declaredStoreReceiptV1`): `check`, `test` and `lint` publish a verdict, not store content, and `publish` and the course `build` list other outputs or none. Alternating `prototype` and `release` runs of one target share one receipt file (its path has no mode), so a switch is expected to rerun the target once (inferred from the path rule, not observed). Not yet verified: no run has exercised the repair path (first proof: run `@teaching/proctor:build` twice, delete the compiler `published/<hash>` slot, run again and expect one `[store-receipt] re-executing` line, with `--tool=nx` and `--tool=bazel`).

The Bazel implementation decides per action from `cache`, `continuous` and `outputs` and is stricter about missing declarations (see its cache decision below).

## 🔌️ Nx implementation

Nx is the default (`defaultTool`). The repository does not use Nx project discovery; it feeds the neutral graph into Nx.

- **Plugin.** `nx.json` registers the plugin `@repo/emoji-project-json` (`📚️library/🟨️.mjs`) for `📋️project.json`. Its `createNodesV2` publishes the neutral graph as Nx projects and its `createDependencies` publishes the neutral edges; it refuses edges whose projects it does not know. Each target is projected onto an Nx executor and receives a private engine identity as an additional input, so a change of the executing implementation invalidates cached results.
- **Executors.** `@semio-tech/repo-lib:command` and `@semio-tech/repo-lib:owner-command` (`📚️library/📦️packages/🟦️typescript/📋️executors.json`) `command` runs the literal command arguments through the original process authority and the Bun shell, `owner-command` forwards the literal argv to the native or process owner without shell interpolation (descriptions in `executors.json`); `repo:noop` becomes `nx:noop`.
- **Translation.** `--skip-cache` becomes `--skip-nx-cache`, `--exclude-dependencies` becomes `--excludeTaskDependencies`, `--output` becomes `--output-style`, `--bail` becomes `--nx-bail`, `--dry-run` and a bare `--graph` (or `--graph=true`) become `--graph=stdout`, `--graph=<file>` is kept. `exec` runs the command in a Bun wrapper that copies the names Nx's own `exec` exports (`NX_PROJECT_NAME`, `NX_PROJECT_ROOT_PATH`) to the neutral `SEMIO_PROJECT_NAME` and `SEMIO_PROJECT_ROOT`; the Nx names exist only in this implementation. `affected` without a selector adds `--base=HEAD`, `--uncommitted --untracked` becomes `--base=HEAD`, `--all` becomes `run-many`. `exec --dry-run` prints the neutral `repo-exec` plan and runs nothing.
- **Boundary.** Production code outside `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap` never loads an Nx module. It goes through the typed port `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🛠️tools/🔌️sdk`, whose closed catalogue names the 13 Nx modules the repository may touch. A test scans every production file under `🦑️repo/🔨️modules` with an own tokenizer and the TypeScript compiler as an independent oracle and requires zero offenders.
- **Cache location.** Nx task results live in `.🧬semio/🦑️repo/⚡️cache/rust/fixed/m/nx/artifacts` (`cacheDirectory` in `nx.json`; the rust partition, 16 GiB, which also holds the shared Cargo target and build directories of `.cargo/config.toml`). The separate partition `n` (`.cache/b/n`, 1.5 GiB) is the Nx pool: it holds the Nx graph cache as funded child images (`⚡️caching/💾️storage/🔒️enforcement/🔌️nx/📦️graph`), not the task results. `repo cache status` walks the artifact directory and prints `CacheStatus`; `cache clear` runs `nx reset --onlyCache` inside the bounded namespace and prints `CacheClear`.
- **Not yet verified.** The whole flow (a real cold run that fills `nx/artifacts`, then a warm run served from it) is scripted but has not been run at the time of writing; the unit and contract tests of the pieces above passed on 2026-10-19 (`⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🛠️tools/⚙️operations`, `🔌️sdk`, `🔁️probe`, `🏗️build/🧭️selection`, `🏗️build/🧭️invocation`: 23 tests, 0 failures). On 2026-10-20 the link `.nx/installation` pointed to a tree under `.cache/b/t/m/nx` that no longer exists (only `.stage-copy-*` leftovers), so every Nx call needs `repo tools setup nx` first.

## 🏗️ Bazel implementation

`--tool=bazel` (or `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL=bazel`) selects Bazel 8.4.2 (`.bazelversion`, pinned download per OS and CPU). The adapter is `📚️library/🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel`. It turns the selected tasks into real Bazel actions, so Bazel's own action cache, disk cache, optional remote cache and lazy evaluation do the work.

- **Generated workspace.** For each distinct selection the adapter writes a workspace under `<products>/bazel/workspaces/<namespace>`: `MODULE.bazel`, a `BUILD.bazel` file, the rules in `tasks.bzl` (`repo_task`, `repo_launch`, `repo_noop`), a task manifest `<label>.json` and a fingerprint `<label>.input` per action, and `s`, a link to the real checkout (a junction on Windows). A task label is `t_` plus 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the task id.
- **Minimal builds.** Only the selected tasks and the tasks reachable through `dependsOn` become actions, and the build is `bazel build` of exactly the selected labels; pure ordering tasks (`repo:noop`) vanish behind their producers unless they are selected themselves. A target that is not needed is never analyzed or run. A selected cached `test` task without declared outputs and without selected dependents runs as a Bazel test with cached test results (see Task dispositions in the Build tools guide).
- **Declared inputs.** The `inputs` of a cacheable task (named groups, `^` expansion, globs, `!` exclusions, `externalDependencies` as `bun.lock` and `package.json`, `dependentTasksOutputFiles` as the producers' outputs) are resolved against the files of the checkout and declared as Bazel inputs: the files reach the action through shared `filegroup`s of labels `s/<path>` instead of one list per task: a directory whose files are all selected is one group `d_<hash>` (a project root is one group, single-child chains collapse), pattern units with more than two members are shared composites `k_<hash>`, and each task gets one composite `i_<hash>` (`bazelSourceCoverV1`; names are ASCII, emoji appear only in file labels). On the static graph of 1,466 projects this cut `BUILD.bazel` from 317 MB to 65 MB and workspace generation from 351 s to 37 s. `glob()` was tried and rejected: with `.bazelignore` it matches nothing, and without it follows symlink loops and git-ignored trees that Nx never hashes. `^name` and `dependencies: true` reach the transitive dependency closure, as Nx hashes them (`bazelTaskInputsV1`); a `dependsOn` that names an absent project or target is refused when the plan is built (`requireBazelDependencyV1`, "Unknown build task dependency") and `bazelDanglingDependenciesV1` lists all of them at once. Paths Bazel cannot name as labels (label-unsafe names, directories, dangling links, anything below a nested `BUILD` package) are hashed into the fingerprint instead. The fingerprint also carries the host platform and CPU, the Bun version, the build mode, declared environment values and `runtime` command outputs. The action manifest is content-stable: it names the task and the executing implementation but no workspace, runtime or snapshot path; the live data sits in `runtime.json`, outside every key.
- **Command closures.** Closure markers (`SEMIO_SOURCE_CLOSURE_<key>`, `SEMIO_CARGO_LOCK_<key>`) are expanded to the files they name before a declaration or a fingerprint is built (`📚️library/🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/🔗️closure`): `bazelClosureMarkersForPlanV1` collects the markers a plan can reach (task inputs, `^` edges and `{input, projects}` references), the manifests are fetched by content address (`managedBazelClosureFetchV1` through the graph store reader of the Nx hasher; a filesystem variant for tests), validated against a closed schema, and names that are absolute or contain `..` are refused. A marker met without loaded manifests is an error, not an empty list. Before this, no closure file reached a Bazel action key, so editing an imported module left a task cached (measured on 2026-10-20 in a scratch run: 106 of 111 modules of one script and 162 of 169 of another were undeclared). Checked with the real Bazel 8.4.2 in the test of that component (`🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/🧪️tests/📥️closure`, 9 tests passed on the live tree on 2026-10-20): a rule over the expanded labels executes once, stays at once after a change outside the closure and executes again after a change to an imported module. Not yet verified: the production fetch under the Bazel storage lease, and the re-measurement of the undeclared modules after the change.
- **Source link and `.bazelignore`.** The generated workspace links the checkout as `s` (`linkBazelSourcesV1`) and writes no `.bazelignore`; it removes a stale one. An earlier adapter listed the link there so that `//...` would not follow it without end, and a scratch run on 2026-10-20 showed the price: `s` was missing from the execroot symlink forest, so Bazel could not stat the declared inputs after a non-sandboxed action and skipped uploading every result (`Skipping uploading outputs because of concurrent modifications`); the disk cache stayed empty and every warm run executed again. Without the ignore, `//...` would follow the link without end, so queries name the root package with `//:all` (every task rule lives in it; 0.4 s in the scratch run). The adapter change was applied on 2026-10-20 at 11:36. Not yet verified: a production `repo --tool=bazel run` that shows the disk cache filling and a warm hit under the corrected adapter; the table below was recorded without the `.bazelignore`; the first scratch runs, with it, cached nothing.
- **Declared outputs.** Each task has exactly one declared tree-artifact output `<label>.snapshot`. A consumer receives the snapshots of its producers (the transitive closure when its input says `transitive: true`) as inputs. After the build the snapshots of the selected tasks are restored into the checkout; intermediate snapshots are restored only inside the consuming action.
- **Cache decision (fail-closed).** Each action carries `cache` and, when it is uncached, a machine-readable reason; the Starlark rule fails without one and tags the action `no-cache`. The adapter decides in this order (`bazelCacheDecisionV1`):
  1. An explicit `cache: true` against a plugin class other than `deterministic` is a conflict and throws ("Conflicting cache declaration"), unless the task is a pure ordering task.
  2. `continuous` (a service) is uncached with reason `continuous`.
  3. `--skip-cache` is uncached with reason `skipped`.
  4. A pure ordering task (`repo:noop`) is always cacheable.
  5. A class other than `deterministic` (see Cache classes) is uncached with the class name as reason: `driver-owned`, `managed-output`, `side-effecting`, `live`, `aggregate`, `diagnostic`, `unclassified`.
  6. `cache: false` in the manifest is `declared` and wins even against a `deterministic` class.
  7. No `cache: true` and no `deterministic` class is `undeclared`: the task reruns until it declares one.
  8. `cache: true` or a `deterministic` class without an `outputs` array is `unbounded`: a hit could not restore what it produced.
  9. Everything else is cacheable.

  So declare `outputs` (`[]` for pure checks) on every cacheable target. On the static graph of 2026-10-20, before the owners' fix, 809 targets in 278 projects declared `cache: true` without `outputs` and therefore ran uncached under Bazel with the reason `unbounded`; `bazelUncachedReportV1` (closed schema `UncachedReport`) groups them per owning project, and no CLI command prints that report yet. Continuous tasks become `repo_launch` actions that run with `bazel run`, are waited for by their readiness declaration and share the build's server.
- **Execution requirements.** Every task and launcher action is tagged `no-remote-exec` (the `repo_noop` marker writes a file and runs no process). Actions that carry `host_state` are also tagged `no-sandbox`; today that is every task and launcher action, only the `repo_noop` marker is sandboxable (`STARLARK_RULES` in `🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/📜️script.ts`; `Actions.kinds` in `🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/🧬️schema/🔣️.json`). The two reasons the schema records (`ActionPolicy.kinds`): a *task* action runs the task process in the real checkout, with its working directory, declared source-tree and shared-store writes, the loopback control socket and the out-of-tree runtime channel, which a sandbox would hide or block; a *launcher* action binds exact execroot output paths and obtains authority over the invocation's control socket, which a sandbox execroot would change. Consequence: results are keyed by the declared inputs and fingerprint, not by what a sandbox observed, so a missing input declaration shows up as a stale hit, not as a failure. The repository `.bazelrc` sets no `--spawn_strategy`, `--sandbox_*` or `--strategy` flag today (the test "the repository .bazelrc carries exactly the authored static cache policy and no host paths" in `🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/💾️storage/🧪️tests` pins its cache lines and rejects host paths, but nothing forbids a sandbox flag), so any action that is ever declared without `host_state` runs in Bazel's default sandbox.
- **Sandbox and host paths.** Bazel 8.4.2 on macOS handles non-ASCII paths of its own state incorrectly, in two places found by experiment: an emoji or accented `--install_base` crashes the server at startup ("Could not find or load main class"), and the `darwin-sandbox` profile is written from Bazel's byte-wise path strings but encodes each byte as UTF-8 a second time, so the write rules for a non-ASCII `--output_base` never match and every sandboxed action (including its `stats.out`) fails with "Operation not permitted". It looks location dependent only because `/private/tmp`, `/private/var/tmp`, `TMPDIR` and `~/Library/Caches` are allow-listed for any spelling. The production layout keeps the `install` base, the `outputs` root and the `server` output bases (`<products>/bazel/outputs/server/<hash>`) inside the native partitions below `.cache/b`, which is ASCII whenever the checkout path is ASCII (for example `/Users/<name>/Documents/semio`), and `requireBazelHostPathsV1` (called by `bazelCacheLayoutV1` in `🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/💾️storage/🟦️.ts`, which every Bazel storage user builds) refuses a layout whose `install`, `outputs` or `server` path contains a non-ASCII character on darwin ("ASCII Bazel host path required on darwin: outputs"), at layout time instead of during a build. So keep the checkout path ASCII on macOS. `--sandbox_base` is not used. A sandboxed action can only write inside its execroot, the temp directories and `~/Library/{Caches,Logs,Developer}`; if a task that writes into the checkout or a partition is ever sandboxed it needs `--sandbox_writable_path=<ASCII path>`, which goes through the same encoder and so must be ASCII too. Linux was examined on 2026-10-20 (Bazel 8.4.2 linux/arm64 in an unprivileged container): a non-ASCII `--install_base` crashes the server there too ("Could not find or load main class", exit code 37) under every locale tried (unset, POSIX, C, C.UTF-8, en_US.UTF-8), so `requireBazelHostPathsV1` refuses a non-ASCII `install` path on Linux ("ASCII Bazel host path required on linux: install", `HostPaths.platforms.linux.guarded`), whereas a non-ASCII output base builds and caches on Linux and is not refused. Windows is covered by the readiness refusal for non-ASCII workspace and partition paths.
- **Emoji source labels.** Task sources are labelled `s/<path>` (`bazelSourceLabelV1`): the file reached through the `s` link, a target name inside the root package. Observed with the pinned 8.4.2 (the real-binary test in `🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/🧪️tests/📥️actions` replays the fixture names and is skipped without `SEMIO_TEST_BAZEL_BINARY`): Bazel accepts any non-ASCII character in a target name, including astral emoji, ZWJ sequences, skin tones, combining marks, CJK and a no-break space, also as action input and output names; only a backslash and control characters are rejected. Only package names must be ASCII, so the adapter never needs an emoji package; it treats a directory that holds `BUILD` or `BUILD.bazel` as a package boundary whatever its spelling and hashes everything below it (`bazelSourceClassesV1`). The adapter admits a path as a label when it matches `^(?:[A-Za-z0-9_@=,+~%./\-]|[^\u0000-\u007f])+$` (code point based, so it holds in Python, Rust and Go too) and hashes everything else (space, backslash, control characters, directories, dangling links, anything below a directory that holds `BUILD`) into the fingerprint instead. In a scratch experiment the Bazel disk cache and the cache keys were identical across `LANG=C` and a UTF-8 locale (no test covers it); the adapter sets a UTF-8 locale on darwin only (`LANG` and `LC_ALL` `en_US.UTF-8`, the `environment` of the darwin row of `PlatformRules`). On Linux arm64 the same emoji source, output and tree-artifact names built under every locale tried (unset, POSIX, C, C.UTF-8, en_US.UTF-8), so Linux needs no locale variable; Windows is `unverified` in the profile.
- **Disk cache.** `<products>/bazel/actions`, with garbage collection (`--experimental_disk_cache_gc_max_size` set to 40 percent of the products partition budget, aligned to MiB; `.bazelrc` adds `--experimental_disk_cache_gc_idle_delay=10s` and `--experimental_disk_cache_gc_max_age=30d`). Collection only runs inside a persistent server that has been idle (measured with Bazel 8.4.2: under `--batch` it never ran), so the adapter does not use `--batch` for builds. The repository contents cache keeps entries 3 days (`--repo_contents_cache_gc_max_age`). The module catalog and repository cache live in `<dependencies>/bazel/modules`; builds run with `--repository_disable_download`, so they never fetch modules.
- **Shared output base.** `--output_base` is `<products>/bazel/outputs/server/<first 16 hex of sha256(workspace directory)>`: one server per generated workspace directory (Bazel restarts a server that is moved between workspace directories), shared by the build and every `bazel run` of continuous tasks of that selection. The server idles out after 120 seconds. The disk cache is shared by all selections, which is what makes a second selection reuse the first one's results.
- **Remote cache.** Rendered from the tool-neutral remote cache contract (section "Remote cache"): `--remote_cache`, `--remote_instance_name`, `--[no]remote_upload_local_results`, `--[no]remote_cache_compression`, `--remote_download_outputs`, `--remote_timeout` and `--remote_retries`, and nothing while no endpoint is configured. `--skip-remote-cache` drops it for one invocation; `--skip-cache` adds `--noremote_accept_cached`. The adapter restores task snapshots from `bazel-bin`, so `minimal` downloads are refused. The access token never appears in a flag: a credential helper in the products `tmp` folder holds only the variable name and the token travels in the environment of the Bazel client (`--remote_header` would copy it into Bazel's `java.log` and build event stream).
- **Cache operations.** `repo --tool=bazel cache status` counts the files of the `ac` and `cas` folders of the disk cache and adds the recorded build activity (builds, actions, executed, cache hits), which each build appends to `<products>/bazel/outputs/server/metrics.json` from its build event stream. `cache clear` stops the persistent servers (by removing their pid file), removes their output bases (Bazel's local action cache) and empties the disk cache, so the next build is a miss; the Bazel binary, modules and toolchains stay.
- **Storage.** Bazel needs the tools, dependencies and products partitions (`bazel/bin` and `bazel/install` in tools, `bazel/modules` in dependencies, `bazel/workspaces`, `bazel/outputs`, `bazel/actions` in products). `repo --tool=bazel reset` invalidates the graph and empties `bazel/actions`, `bazel/outputs` (which holds the server output bases and the activity ledger) and `bazel/workspaces`; it does not stop a running Bazel server first, so run it when no build is active (`cache clear` stops them).
- **Platform profile.** Every platform-dependent decision of the adapter is one pure function of observed host facts, `bazelPlatformProfileV1` in `🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/🌐️platform` (rules and limits in `PlatformRules` of the Bazel schema; fixture `🧪️tests/📥️platform/🔣️.json`, 19 rows for darwin, linux, the devcontainer and win32 on x64 and arm64, including the refusals). Per platform it renders: the extra startup and command flags (`--nobuild_runfile_links` everywhere, because nothing runs `bazel run` and a runfiles symlink forest would need a symlink privilege on Windows; `--noenable_runfiles` and the startup option `--nowindows_enable_symlinks` on Windows, so the source link `s` is a junction and no developer mode or elevation is needed), the locale environment (`LANG`/`LC_ALL` `en_US.UTF-8` on darwin only), the launcher extension (`.exe` on Windows), the kind of the source link (`junction` on Windows) and the path budget `len(output base) + len(\execroot\_main\s\) + 256` in UTF-16 units against 259 (MAX_PATH) or, with `LongPathsEnabled`, 32766; a budget that does not fit, a Bun path that is not absolute (and `.exe` on Windows) and a devcontainer flag on a non-Linux host are refusals, thrown before Bazel starts. `--output_user_root` is gone: `--output_base` and `--install_base` are always explicit, so it was dead weight and (Windows) a second long path. Every action runs the Bun executable directly (`ctx.actions.run`, `executable = ctx.attr.bun`), never a shell, `genrule` or `run_shell`; the BUILD quotes the Bun path with `JSON.stringify`, which a real Bazel parses for a Windows path with backslashes. Evidence of 2026-10-20 (Bazel 8.4.2): macOS arm64 and Linux arm64 (container built from `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, user `vscode`, no capability, `.cache` on a Docker ext4 volume): `SEMIO_TEST_BAZEL_BINARY` run of the new suite passed on both (Bazel's own `help` lists every rendered flag name, a BUILD with a Windows Bun path parses, a build of an emoji source through the `s` link with the rendered flags is served from the disk cache after `clean`: `disk cache hit`); the pinned linux/arm64 binary was downloaded and matched its pin; `bun repo --help`, `bun repo preflight` and `bun repo tools setup bazel` (47 s: binary, 156 module blobs, readiness line) ran unprivileged on Linux. Linux findings by experiment: emoji source labels, outputs, tree artifacts and a non-ASCII output base work under every locale tried (unset, POSIX, C, C.UTF-8, en_US.UTF-8), a non-ASCII `--install_base` does not (see Sandbox and host paths), `linux-sandbox` is not registered in an unprivileged container (default seccomp; also not with `seccomp=unconfined`) and Bazel's default `sandboxed` strategy falls back to `processwrapper-sandbox`, which ran a sandboxed action there, while a `--privileged` container registers `linux-sandbox` and ran one; so the adapter names no host-specific sandbox strategy (hermetic actions list the alias `sandboxed`; a platform-specific name that is not registered on the host is refused, as the macOS binary showed for `linux-sandbox`). Not run: Windows (the profile, the refusals and the Starlark escaping are proven by fixture and by the Linux and macOS binaries, not by a Windows binary), the emoji labels on Windows (`unverified` in the profile, one warning per run), whether `bun.exe` is long-path aware, and the CI matrix of `.github/workflows/build-system.yml`.
- **Verified and not yet verified.** Bazel 8.4.2 was exercised on macOS arm64 in scratch workspaces.
